[[sep-dv-verification-plan]]
== SEP OCAH Open-Source Verification Plan

=== Overview

[cols=",",options="header"]
|===
|Field |Value
|Design Under Test |`sep_wrapper` (`hw/top/sep_wrapper.sv` + `sep_ip_integration`)
|Bare core |`sep` (`hw/sys/sep/rtl/sep.sv`)
|Repository |`tt-oca-harness`
|Project |OCAH
|Framework |PyUVM on cocotb (Phase 1, Phase 2 and Phase 3); SystemVerilog UVM smoke set on the same top (`--framework uvm`, VCS)
|Primary simulator |Verilator
|Design specification |`hw/sys/sep/doc/index.adoc` and the chapters it includes
|Register sources |`hw/sys/sep/regs/` (SystemRDL); the generated export `hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/`, read through `cocotb/env/sep_reg_meta.py`
|How to run |`README.md` in this DV tree (`tools/dv/run_dv.py --dut sep`)
|Cocotb tests |Every member of the `all` group in `testlists/all.toml`; its `expected_count` is the authoritative number
|SV-UVM smoke set |Every test with a `uvm` binding (`--framework uvm --items all`); see <<sv-uvm-smoke>>
|===

This VPLAN is the technical contract for the open-source SEP DV
suite: what each phase covers, how tests are named, which VIPs
drive the DUT, and what each registered test must prove. It is
written for adopters and maintainers of the public tree. Testbench
structure (env hierarchy, HDL top, bring-up helpers) is in
<<sep-dv-testbench-architecture,SEP_TB_ARCH>>.

PASS/FAIL needs positive evidence: a passing `results.xml` and named
`CHK-<ID> PASS` lines in the leaf log (<<sep-vplan-evidence-gate>>). A clean
simulator exit alone is not enough.

=== Verification Strategy

One DUT. Three phases on cocotb, plus a SystemVerilog UVM smoke set
(see <<sv-uvm-smoke>>).

[cols="1,2,3",options="header"]
|===
|Phase |Environment |Job

|Phase 1
|cocotb / PyUVM + Verilator
|Smoke plus subsystem representatives. Fewest tests that carry the most real datapath behaviour.

|Phase 2
|Same cocotb environment
|Iconic feature coverage by IP, plus cross-IP edges a per-IP bench cannot see. Includes block activation for instantiated IPs that have no other vehicle. Randomize when there is a legal matrix (`RAND-REP` / `RANDCFG`); keep directed (`RAND-NONE`) when there is not.

|Phase 3
|Same cocotb environment. A test that reads state that only a four-state simulator shows pins VCS.
|Residual contracts: the contracts of the specification and the register description that no Phase 1 or Phase 2 check grades. Corner and illegal cells, injected faults, reset and lock behaviour, and drawn matrices. A Phase 3 test never overlaps a Phase 1 or Phase 2 test. A test that is a superset of an open test names the cell that it adds. Randomize when there is a legal matrix (`RAND-REP` / `RANDCFG`); keep directed (`RAND-NONE`) when there is not. Every expected value comes from the specification and the register description, never from the RTL and never from another test. A check that rests on a PROPOSED internal drive is blocked and takes no credit. A read-only observation tap, an error inject and a drive of a `sep` port are not PROPOSED. A test with no executable check is parked (<<sep-vplan-credit-rules>>, <<sep-vplan-phase3-parked>>).
|===

Phase 1, Phase 2 and Phase 3 are scope tiers, not a schedule. Phase 1 is the
bring-up and representative set. Phase 2 is the dense, breadth and fault set.
Phase 3 is the residual set. Each test entry names its phase. `SEP_FCOV.adoc`
has its own Phase 1 / Phase 2 taxonomy: Phase 1 holds the basic-mode groups and
Phase 2 the dense groups; an FCOV phase does not follow the phase of its owning
test. The FCOV Phase 2 groups are the coverage of the Phase 3 tests. A tier
says what a test is asked to prove. It does not say when the test is written.
A Phase 3 entry takes credit under the rules of <<sep-vplan-credit-rules>>.
The contracts this plan does not claim are in <<sep-vplan-scope>>. The Phase 3
contracts that no test can grade are in <<sep-vplan-phase3-lists>>. The
observation taps, the added hooks and the PROPOSED drives of the Phase 3 tests
are in <<sep-vplan-phase3-proposed>>. The parked-test rule of Phase 3 is in
<<sep-vplan-phase3-parked>>.

[arabic]
. *Stimulus*: no-CPU tests drive the CPU-LSU AXI splice through
  `ocah_axi_vip` / `SepAxiAgent`. An optional second master drives the
  real SMN-inbound port (traverses the inbound filter). CPU tests boot
  VeeR EL2 firmware from ICCM. SPI flash uses `ocah_spi_vip`.
  A test whose target is `lsu_stub_all_live` runs on two builds.
  The Verilator build is `lsu_stub_all_live`: `shims/cpu/sep_cpu_stub.sv`
  replaces `sep_cpu` and drives the LSU request. The VCS coverage build,
  which produces the graded logs, is `--target default`: the full VeeR
  core is held off and `tb/tb_top.sv` force-splices the VIP onto the same
  LSU request node (the no_cpu force-splice block). Both builds present
  the request at that node, so a test whose checked path is downstream of
  it covers both builds. A test whose checked path includes `sep_cpu`
  itself names the build its evidence comes from.
. *Checking*: frontdoor reads/writes, independent Python goldens where
  needed (AES/HMAC/KMAC/OTBN/entropy/LCC), and named checkers that can
  fail on a broken DUT. Prefer source-derived expected values from the
  register export and architecture docs.
. *Bring-up*: drive `ext_boot_seq_done_i`; use `+skip_fuse_sense` only
  when the test does not need real OTP sense; hold the CPU off for
  `no_cpu` runs. With `+skip_fuse_sense` and no
  `+sep_shadow_reg_preload`, the eFuse shadow is all zero except
  `LC_STATE` raw `4'hF`, which is INVALID. `FEAT_CTRL` is then zero, so
  `sep_debug` is 0 and the inbound filter is in the SMN-inbound path. A
  skip-mode test that sends SMN-inbound traffic programs a filter window,
  and a deny check that `FEAT_CTRL` = 0 would also produce does not prove
  a filter rule.
. *Coverage*: the feature matrix (<<iconic-scorecard>>) for the
  cocotb phases. VCS Phase 1 functional-coverage bins live in
  <<sep-dv-functional-coverage,SEP_FCOV>> (`u_sep_fcov`). SystemVerilog covergroups
  are not compiled on the Verilator acceptance path. RTL code coverage
  is a separate, unthresholded number (<<sep-fcov-evidence-paths>>).

Randomization contract: the *same* config object programs the DUT and the
golden. Pin the datapath directed first, then upgrade that test to randomized —
do not ship a one-point stand-in for a matrix. Skip randomization only when the
feature has no legal knob space (a strap, a single named handshake).

[[audit]]
Checker contracts in this plan are written against these known ways a check can
pass while the DUT is unable to fail it.

*An expectation that shares a source with the thing it measures.* A golden must
not be selected by asking the engine which candidate it agrees with. The HMAC
software-key word order is pinned from the IP register specification:
`vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/upstream/hw/ip/hmac/data/hmac.hjson` (the `KEY` multireg) states
`key[1023:0] = {KEY0, KEY1, ... , KEY31}`, so `KEY_0` is the most-significant
word and the software path needs no reversal. The RTL agrees
(`hmac.sv` assigns the key CSRs into the secret-key vector in reverse
index order), and that agreement is corroboration rather than the source: a
golden transcribed from the RTL cannot disagree with the RTL. A two-candidate
scan is legitimate only as a failure diagnostic that separates a word-order
regression from a general digest fault.

*Golden compared against golden.* An assert between a result already pinned
bit-exact to its golden and another output of the same model holds with the
simulator switched off. The degenerate case compares list lengths rather than
domains — a four-word wrong-mode golden against an eight-word digest.

*A guard whose own constructor makes it unfalsifiable.* Non-vacuity must not
rest on a relation the configuration builder already guarantees: an unwritten
(zero-initialised) address required to differ from a pattern built as
`getrandbits(64) | 1`, or `start_lo != 0` where the config builds that value as
`(random & ~0xFFF) or 0x1000`.

*A stimulus with no fan-out.* Driving a pin that is unread in the configuration
under test and asserting that two observables did not move is a DV-invented
scenario, not a specification claim. `dbg_rstb_i` is routed only into
`sep_cpu`, so it has no observable in a CPU-stub run, and
`hw/sys/sep/doc/port_table.adoc` does not require isolation from system reset.

*A tolerance wider than the signal.* A post-event bound above the count the
window actually reaches passes when the event is ignored. The window must run
long enough that the pre-event count clears the tolerance by a wide margin.

*A check that reads back nothing.* Walking a register through several encodings
without reading it back cannot distinguish a register stuck at its reset value
when those encodings produce byte-identical output for the chosen stimulus.

*Entailed guards.* The absence of a complementary-arm marker, or a deferred
line, is not a checker; the owning test already fails if a forbidden marker
appears.

[[sep-vplan-evidence-gate]]
==== Evidence gate

Per-run logs land in `build/runs/<run-id>/` (git-ignored). PASS/FAIL requires
positive evidence from `results.xml`. A clean simulator exit alone is not
enough. A test passing is the entry condition for reading its checkers, never a
substitute for them. A checker row exists only if a run can prove it. A log tag
is not the proof.

A test that exits cleanly without checking anything is not a pass, and
`sep_base_test` makes such a run fail. Every check logs `CHK-<ID> PASS`. The
base class counts the distinct IDs that a leaf emits and prints one line per
test:

----
EVIDENCE_SUMMARY test=<name> observed=N own=N required=N missing=N ids=...
----

`own` is the observed set less the IDs in `_EvidenceFilter.BASE_IDS`. That set
is empty, so every record counts toward `own`, the shared records listed under
Test Procedures included. A leaf whose `own` count is zero fails. Firmware
leaves emit `CHK-FW-CONSOLE` from `poll_boot` after the mailbox PASS magic, or
`CHK-VERDICT` after `cold_scratch[0]` reports completion on the scratch0-gated
leaves. `_EvidenceFilter.NO_OWN_EVIDENCE` names the leaves that may pass with no
own record. It is empty, and a new entry hides a logging gap.

A leaf can declare more: `min_evidence = N` sets a floor on `own`, and
`required_evidence = ("CHK-A", ...)` names IDs that must appear.

The gate proves that a check ran. It does not prove that the check was right.

[[bench-assertion-waiver]]
==== Bench-scope assertion waiver

The `[pass_fail]` patterns of `sep_sim_cfg.toml` decide, for every leaf, which
assertion failure the runner reads as a failure. They answer for the whole
bench rather than for one test, so they take this record rather than a run-mode
note on a test entry. A per-test assertion waiver stays a plusarg on its test entry.

[cols="1,4",options="header"]
|===
|Field |Value
|Claim |A leaf graded PASS has had every assertion failure of the run read, apart from the time-0 firing of the two external-ack assertions of the vendored Adams Bridge register block and any other `abr_reg.sv` message at time 0
|Why |`vendor/chipsalliance/adams-bridge/upstream/src/abr_top/rtl/abr_reg.sv` holds two immediate assertions on `external_wr_ack` and `external_rd_ack`, in the post-reset branch of an `always_ff`. On a four-state simulator the first clock edge sees the reset and the ack strobes at X, and each assertion fires once at time 0. The file is vendored third-party RTL, so the tree does not edit it, and `$assertoff` in the testbench races the time-0 evaluation
|Scope |Two `extra_ignore_patterns` entries and one `extra_hard_fail_patterns` entry. The ignore entries drop an `abr_reg.sv` line that reports time 0 and a line that names `abr_reg.sv` line 67 or 69. The hard-fail entry matches the timestamped line of the same two assertions with a non-zero `failed at` time. The runner matches hard-fail patterns over the raw log, whatever the ignore patterns drop, so a later firing fails the leaf. No other assertion is touched
|Closes when |The vendored block stops asserting on an X ack strobe, or the reset and the ack strobes into it are at known values at the first clock edge. Either removes the three patterns
|===

Re-confirm: `grep -nF 'abr_reg\.sv' hw/sys/sep/dv/sep_sim_cfg.toml` prints
exactly three pattern lines: two under `extra_ignore_patterns` and one under
`extra_hard_fail_patterns` that carries `failed at [1-9]`. Any other output
means that the record is stale, and the record is what needs the edit.

=== Naming Rules

[cols=",",options="header"]
|===
|Rule |Requirement
|Test case name |`sep_<feature>_<scenario>_test` in lower snake case
|Single-scenario module |Module name matches the test case
|Plusarg-selected variant |Several testlist entries may share one module when each selects its scenario with a plusarg. The entry name then does NOT end the module name, so the entry must carry the plusarg in `args` and a comment naming the scenario. The variants in the tree are `sep_efuse_secure_tm_write_lock_test` (`efuse.sep_locked_field_access_irq_path_test`, `+secure_tm_lock`), `sep_esrc_e2e_fifo_closed_test` (`system.sep_esrc_e2e_smoke_test`, `+esrc_fifo_closed`) and the seven `sep_lcc_lc_state_w1s_*_test` (`lcc.sep_lcc_lc_state_transition_matrix_test`, `+lc_start=N`). Use it to avoid copying one implementation per scenario, not to hide a missing module. A Phase 3 test has one testlist entry, and a second one only when the run mode differs (<<sep-vplan-phase3-drawn>>)
|Multi-scenario / RAND-REP |One leaf test walks every required discrete cell in one invocation; do not silently ship a one-point stand-in for a matrix. A Phase 3 leaf walks only cheap, contract-critical cells and draws the other cells from its seed (<<sep-vplan-phase3-drawn>>)
|Open-source files |PyUVM test under `cocotb/tests/`, stimulus in `cocotb/seq_lib/`, shared helpers on `sep_base_test` / `cocotb/env/`
|Testlist enrollment |Leaf entry in the owning `testlists/*.toml` and in `testlists/all.toml`
|Evidence |Every checker needs a positive log line naming the contract, as `CHK-<ID> PASS`
|Evidence gate |A clean exit is not a pass: every graded contract logs `CHK-<ID> PASS`, and `sep_base_test` fails a leaf that graded nothing of its own. <<sep-vplan-evidence-gate>> describes the mechanism, its `EVIDENCE_SUMMARY` line, the `min_evidence` / `required_evidence` knobs and the exemption list
|===

=== BFM Policy

[cols=",",options="header"]
|===
|Interface / Protocol |Open-Source Replacement
|CPU-LSU AXI (no_cpu) |`ocah_axi_vip` via `SepAxiAgent` on the LSU splice
|SMN-inbound AXI |Second `SepAxiAgent` on the real inbound port
|SMN-outbound AXI |`tb/sep_outbound_mbx.sv`: firmware console and PASS/FAIL mailbox monitor. It answers every outbound access OKAY, and every read with zero data, for any address. A test whose check depends on an outbound response names it under *BFM / Model*.
|SPI flash |`ocah_spi_vip` `OcahSpiFlash`
|JTAG (eFuse mux path) |`ocah_axi_vip` AXI-Lite master on the OTP JTAG2AXIL port (`j_axi_*`)
|Entropy noise / goldens |TB noise port + pure-Python goldens in `cocotb/env/`
|eFuse / OTP |Generic OSS eFuse model (no foundry OTP)
|Memories |Wrapper macros + sim-time image load
|===

[[sep-vplan-scope]]
=== Verification Scope

This plan claims SEP behaviour at the `sep_wrapper` boundary of the
open-source build: the Bender targets `sep`, `sep_el2` and `sep_wrapper`, with
the DV shims and models that <<sep-dv-testbench-architecture,SEP_TB_ARCH>>
describes. The behaviours below are outside this plan. A green SEP regression
says nothing about them.

[cols="1,2",options="header"]
|===
|Behaviour |Why it is out of scope here
|Boot ROM firmware contracts: the `rom_fw` and `rom_fw_smoke` groups, `testlists/rom_fw.toml` |They have their own owner, the `rom_boot` RTL target and the `boot_rom*` firmware profiles, and they are firmware contracts rather than hardware contracts. They are not members of `all`, so this plan grades none of them
|Proprietary IP |Excluded for OSS hygiene: proprietary IPs outside the public tree. The build uses the Bender targets `sep`, `sep_el2` and `sep_wrapper` only
|Inbound global-to-local remap |`u_inbound_global_to_local_addr_remap` (`hw/sys/sep/rtl/sep_system_peripherals/rtl/sep_system_peripherals.sv`) rewrites inbound addresses in [`SEP_GLOBAL_BASE_ADDR`, +`SEP_REGION_SIZE`) to local addresses after `u_inbound_filter`. No test in `all` grades the translation. `sep_fabric_remap_filter_csr_bank_test` grades the CSR read/write path. The sixteen programmable `axi_alias_remap` regions are a different mechanism, which `sep_fabric_local_alias_datapath_test` grades
|Reset generation and return after a watchdog bite |The integrating reset controller outside this DUT owns them. `sep_reset_wdt_sanity_test` grades the bite request at the SEP output pin
|Entropy quality, ring-oscillator tuning and health tests over real noise |The bench injects noise at the decorrelator input (<<esrc-noise-exception>>). These belong to the entropy IP's own testbench
|Features with no vehicle in this suite: AGP, local UART/GPIO, ROM patch, DMA-AES, DCLS-in-SEP, PCR vault |No test in `all` drives them, and the feature matrix (<<iconic-scorecard>>) has no row for them
|Entropy-pool drain-under-fill |No test in `all` drives it. `sep_entropy_pool_aperture_test` grades the stall duration (`CHK-STALL-DURATION`)
|Drain-before-reset on the AES and OTBN isolation domains |No test in `all` drives it. The HMAC, KMAC, Adams Bridge and shared TRNG domains are graded (<<iconic-scorecard>>)
|Locked-field access interrupt taken by the CPU PIC and its service routine |No test in `all` drives it. `sep_locked_field_access_irq_path_test` grades the interrupt line
|ESRC health-test quality, and the `prim_count` counter-redundancy fault |No test in `all` drives them. The `prim_count` error is reachable only by a physical fault, so it needs fault injection (<<sep_esrc_alert_delivery_test>>)
|Inbound-filter negative product |No test in `all` drives it
|Negative matrices for the CPU LSU, the fabric gaps, the extended IRQ connectivity and the eFuse permissions |No test in `all` drives them
|KMAC sideload fail-closed `START` with no valid key (`ErrKeyNotValid`), and recovery through `CMD.err_processed` |No test in `all` drives it (<<sep_km_kmac_sideload_kat_test>>)
|===

[[sep-vplan-traceability]]
=== Scope and Traceability

This section is the requirement-to-test-to-coverage matrix for SEP. The
requirements are the designer-owned design documentation under
`hw/sys/sep/doc/`. The Test Procedures below state what each test verifies,
`SEP_FCOV.adoc` states the coverage intent, and the live group membership is
`testlists/all.toml` and the per-area lists it includes. This section
reconciles them: which tests count as feature credit, and which features have
no credit on this bench.

[[sep-vplan-source-of-truth]]
==== Source of truth

The DV book carries no specification or register reference of its own. Where
the documents overlap, the table names the one that wins.

[cols="2,3,3",options="header"]
|===
|Question |Source of truth |Disposition of the other documents
|What SEP must do |The design documentation under `hw/sys/sep/doc/` |This plan cites it. A plan statement that contradicts a design chapter is a plan defect
|Register layouts |The SystemRDL under `hw/sys/sep/regs/` and the generated export under `hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/` |`cocotb/env/sep_reg_meta.py` reads the generated export, and the register metadata that the tests use comes from it
|Which tests exist |`testlists/all.toml` and the per-area lists it includes (`system.toml`, `cpu.toml`, `memory.toml`, `km.toml`, `crypto.toml`, `efuse_lcc.toml`, `spi.toml`, `rom_fw.toml`) |The Test Procedures document every member of `all`. A name in one and not the other is a defect in whichever lacks it, except a Phase 3 entry whose test is not in `all`: that entry takes no credit (<<sep-vplan-credit-rules>>)
|Which tests count |The `all` group, pinned by its `expected_count` in `testlists/all.toml` |`smoke`, `cpu_stub` and `cpu` are subsets of `all`. `rom_fw` and `rom_fw_smoke` are outside this plan (<<sep-vplan-scope>>)
|Coverage intent per requirement |`SEP_FCOV.adoc` and `cov/sv/sep_fcov.sv` |The matrix names the covergroups that a row leans on. The bin definitions stay in `SEP_FCOV.adoc`
|Code-coverage points that cannot be hit |The exclusion lists and the scope file under `cov/config/vcs/` |`SEP_FCOV.adoc` (<<sep-fcov-closure>>) states the RTL fact behind each list. This matrix repeats none of them
|===

[[sep-vplan-live-groups]]
==== Live groups versus credit

Executable membership comes from the testlists. The `expected_count` guard of
`all` pins its size.

[cols="1,4",options="header"]
|===
|Group |Role
|`smoke` |Pull-request gate (`sim.yml`): `sep_axi_smoke_test`. Bring-up, not a credit set
|`cpu_stub` |Runtime class: the `no_cpu` members of `all`. A partition of `all`, not a credit set of its own
|`cpu` |Runtime class: the firmware-boot members of `all`. A partition of `all`, not a credit set of its own
|`all` |The credit denominator: every test that this plan grades, the union of `cpu_stub` and `cpu`
|`rom_fw`, `rom_fw_smoke` |Outside this plan. No credit
|===

[[sep-vplan-credit-rules]]
==== Credit rules

* Feature credit requires a test in `all`, the named evidence that its entry
  declares (`CHK-<ID> PASS` lines, <<sep-vplan-evidence-gate>>), and a positive
  `results.xml`. A failing test gives no credit.
* A row names the tests whose checkers prove that feature, and states in the
  row any part of the feature those tests leave unproven. Feature rows are
  independent of per-test checkers.
* The architecture specification under `hw/sys/sep/doc/` is the denominator: a
  feature that the specification does not define is not a row. An *iconic* row
  is the mode, command, or contract that makes that IP itself.
* A feature takes credit once, at the earliest phase that proves it. A later
  test that also reaches the feature does not take the credit again.
* A known RTL defect or specification gap that the suite exercises is a
  <<known-limitations>> row, not a feature credit.
* The cocotb and SV-UVM realizations of one name are one scenario and take
  credit once (<<sv-uvm-smoke>>).
* Coverage credit is the covergroup bins of `cov/sv/sep_fcov.sv`, collected on
  VCS. A bin records that an interface event happened, never that it was
  correct. Correctness stays with the checkers.
* A Phase 3 entry (<<sep-vplan-phase3>>) takes credit only when its test is in
  `all`, the named evidence that its entry declares (`CHK-<ID> PASS` lines) is
  in the leaf log, and `results.xml` is positive.
* [[sep-vplan-reserved-bits]]Every graded register compare of a Phase 3 entry
  (an expected value from the RDL or a model, a read-back, a before and after
  compare, or a compare of two reads) uses the RDL field bits of the register
  only. A reserved bit is graded only where the RDL or the specification states
  its value. A reserved bit with no stated value is masked out of the compare,
  and the PASS or FAIL line logs the masked bits and the mask.
* The Phase 3 entries, the Phase 3 lists and `SEP_FCOV.adoc` use four class
  words and four terms for bench items, with these meanings:
** *PROPOSED*: a hook that drives, forces or deposits into a net or a state
   inside the `sep` module for a purpose other than error injection, and that
   is not in `hw/sys/sep/dv/tb/tb_top.sv` and not in the closed set of
   `docs/SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc` (<<sep-vplan-phase3-proposed>>).
** *Observation tap*: a read-only observation that drives nothing: a testbench
   variable, an XMR probe port, an observation `bind` or a hierarchical read
   reference of a DUT net. An observation tap is not PROPOSED and blocks no
   check, leg or bin. A tap that `docs/SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc` does not list is an
   observation tap the test adds to the SEP_TB_ARCH observation-probe list
   (<<sep-vplan-phase3-proposed>>).
** *Added hook*: an error inject or a top-port drive that `docs/SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc`
   does not list and that `hw/sys/sep/dv/tb/tb_top.sv` does not have. An error
   inject is an inject or a force that puts a fault or
   an illegal condition into the DUT, at a net inside the `sep` module or
   outside it. A top-port drive is a drive of a port of the `sep` module by the
   bench: a stream input, a strap, an SMC, JTAG, DMI, DFT or debug port, a
   memory-macro response port, or a port value that a plusarg sets. An added
   hook is not PROPOSED and blocks no check, leg or bin. The test adds an error
   inject to the inject exceptions of `docs/SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc` and a top-port
   drive to the port table of its HDL Top section (<<sep-vplan-phase3-proposed>>).
** *Existing bench hook*: a drive, a force, an inject or a time-0 plusarg that
   `hw/sys/sep/dv/tb/tb_top.sv` has. It is existing DV infrastructure and is
   approved, whatever net it drives and whatever its purpose: it is not
   PROPOSED, it is not an added hook, and it blocks no check, leg or bin. When
   `docs/SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc` does not list it, the test that uses it adds its row
   to `docs/SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc`, and <<sep-vplan-phase3-added-hooks>> names it with
   the kind "Existing bench hook".
** *Preload*: the content of a memory macro outside `sep` that the approved
   image load `tb_backdoor_mem` of `hw/sys/sep/dv/tb/tb_top.sv` puts in place at
   t=0, before the macro is read: a Key Manager ROM image, its parameter block or
   a `rom_main` build, loaded through `+km_rom_hex`. A preload is part of the
   approved t=0 image load. It is not a hook, it is not PROPOSED, and it blocks
   no check, leg or bin. A test that uses a new `rom_main` build adds the build
   to the section "Key Manager `rom_main` Firmware Builds" of
   `docs/SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc` (<<sep-vplan-km-proposed>>).
** *Blocked*: a check, leg or coverage bin that needs a PROPOSED item, or a
   value that the specification or the register description does not state
   (<<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked>>). An entry writes `Blocked: <item or value>`
   at the check, leg or bin: "Blocked: needs the PROPOSED drive `x`" at a check,
   leg or bin, or "Blocked: the specification does not state X". A blocked feature is a feature whose checks all need such
   a value. A blocked check, leg or bin takes no credit and is not in an
   executable total.
** *Executable*: a Phase 3 test with at least one graded check that is not
   blocked. A coverage bin is executable when it is not blocked and an
   executable check of an executable owning test fills it. A coverpoint or
   group is executable when every bin is executable, and *executable in part*
   when some bins are executable and some are blocked.
** *Parked*: a Phase 3 test with no executable check
   (<<sep-vplan-phase3-parked>>), or a Phase 2 coverpoint or group with no
   executable bin (<<sep-fcov-parked,SEP_FCOV>>). The entry of a parked test
   has the field `*Needs*: <PROPOSED items or unstated values>`. A parked test
   takes no credit and is not in an executable total. A coverpoint with no
   executable bin is written "parked: <item or value>", for example "parked:
   needs the PROPOSED drive `x`".
* The behaviours of <<sep-vplan-scope>> take no credit.

[[iconic-scorecard]]
==== Matrix

One row per iconic feature. *Tests* links each owning test to its entry.
*Groups* lists the testlist groups of those tests. *Coverage* names the
Phase 1 covergroups that list a test of the row as an owner
(<<sep-fcov-owners>>); "None" means that no test of the row owns a Phase 1
bin. *Checker evidence* states what the checkers of those tests prove and what
they leave out; the checker table of each linked entry is the full set.

While HMAC is held, the same `DIGEST_0` read and a `CFG` write complete SLVERR, the read does not return the live digest, and AES stays OKAY. A software reset of OTBN, AES, or KMAC cancels only that engine's EDN endpoints: its outstanding `edn_req` drops with no `edn_ack`, and a sibling client is still granted afterwards. The cancel precedes the engine's gated reset by at least one cycle, a word the endpoint held is never acknowledged to any client afterwards, and a KMAC reset stepped across one URND acknowledge period keeps the KMAC endpoint's `edn_ack` and `edn_bus` at 0 while URND completes its own beat. Drain-before-reset is claimed for the HMAC, KMAC and Adams Bridge domains — see the detail entry; it is not claimed for AES or OTBN. The ABR domain also carries the JTAG `IC_RESET` override check, and its live-result behaviour is graded on `sep_abr_mlkem_kat_test`. |SEC_DIS override |<<sep_sec_dis_override_test,`sep_sec_dis_override_test`>> |all, cpu_stub |`sep_feat_ctrl_cg`, `sep_sec_dis_boot_cg` |`CHK-OVR-ALONE` grades the `sep_reset_n` override before any match: reset releases and feature control stays closed (`hw/sys/sep/doc/security_disable.adoc`). `CHK-SENSE-GATED-RESET` grades that the match leaves the reset held. `CHK-FEAT-PRESENSE` grades that the match opens feature control before sensing finishes (`hw/sys/sep/doc/token_processing.adoc`). `CHK-OVR-AFTER` grades the same override inputs after the match. `CHK-MAP-OPEN` requires an OKAY `LC_STATE` shadow-map read while sensing is still open. `CHK-SENSE-STUCK` grades that the match stops the sense. The `CHK-RETAIN-*` checkers grade the recovery reset: the token digest survives `rst_ni`, so SEC_DIS, the open map and the override still work and sense stays stopped. `CHK-DROP` and `CHK-SENSE-CONTROL` clear SEC_DIS and grade a full sense after a second reset. The post-sense match, mismatch and restore cells also run in this leaf. The leaf drives the SEP override inputs and reads the reset probes. `sep_sec_dis_reset_reach_test` grades the bus access and the IC_RESET TDR write. |YES

Slot coverage does not depend on the seed; only the bit offsets within a spare do. |YES

A redundancy fault forces the reserved error code onto the token status, and the `LC_STATE` bit[1] advance must not land — the gate compares for equality with the match code, so a gate written as "not a mismatch" would let the fault code through. The cell that follows presents the same token with the fault removed and does advance, so the pair is a contrast rather than a lone negative. |YES

The set-only phases prove the fault bits survive a valid retry; only this one separates that from a latch that never clears. The DFT-inserted fuse-path ports are separate DUT outputs, not members of `dbg_disable`: `sep_fuse_dft_disable` is Case 3 AND `sep_fuse_dbg`, inverted; `smc_fuse_dft_disable` is Case 2 AND `smc_fuse_dbg`, inverted. The compose cell closes those two bits while Case 2 and Case 3 stay open. |YES

Every remap / filter slot tail, the last one included, reads zero with OKAY inside the RDL allocation (base + `TOTAL_SIZE`). |YES

Bursts into other register regions are outside the specification and are not graded; see <<known-limitations>>. |YES for the claimed part; the evidence cell names the rest

Pass-through while disabled is the observation aperture, not a graded contract. |YES
|Mailbox outbox FIFO, threshold interrupt and error flags |<<sep_axil_mailbox_iface_rand_test,`sep_axil_mailbox_iface_rand_test`>> |all, cpu_stub |`sep_mbox_rand_cg` |The SEP-host side of the outbound aperture: native 64-bit pushes at one FIFO entry per beat, STATUS compared field-by-field against a golden occupancy model after every push, the write-threshold interrupt status gated by its enable and level-held across write-one-to-clear, read-from-empty and write-to-full each answered with their documented response and error flag, control flush, and the write-only decode of the push register. This aperture's interrupt leaves the block on `smc_mailbox_interrupt_o`, so only its status registers are graded here; `Mailbox -> PIC` grades delivery to the SEP CPU, which the inbound aperture drives, on `sep_mailbox_plic_test`. |Interrupt aggregator packing under simultaneous fan-in |<<sep_irq_simultaneous_fanin_no_alias_test,`sep_irq_simultaneous_fanin_no_alias_test`>> |all, cpu_stub |None |Several cross-IP sources asserted at once must produce exactly their own bits in `sep_internal_interrupts[8:33]`, with every bit of that region resolved rather than floating. `PIC source map` grades the source-to-PIC-id map and CPU delivery on `sep_pic_irq_source_map_delivery_test`; this row is the aggregation itself — the place a one-hot map can smear onto a neighbour — and is a distinct contract from delivery. |YES

|SECURE_TM shadow-write lock |<<sep_efuse_secure_tm_write_lock_test,`sep_efuse_secure_tm_write_lock_test`>> |all, cpu_stub |None |Each of `LOCKS`, `LOCKS_SPARE`, `LC_STATE`, `SIP_DIS` and `SYS_DIS` takes the same seeded payload twice: with the strap low the write lands (set-only rows OR-merge; `LC_STATE[31:8]` takes a plain shadow write), and after the strap is raised and the image re-sensed the stored value does not move and `sep_internal_interrupts[33]` pulses. The write response is logged, not graded. A `SecureTmUnlock` spare still takes a write with the strap up, so the refusal is the lock rather than a dead write path. Real fuse sense at `LC_PROD`. |YES for the claimed part; the evidence cell names the rest
|eFuse locked-field access violation interrupt |<<sep_locked_field_access_irq_path_test,`sep_locked_field_access_irq_path_test`>> |all, cpu_stub |None |A write to a write-locked spare and a read of a read-locked spare each raise `sep_internal_interrupts[33]` (PIC source 34) for the duration of the access and drop the line after the access retires. The write-locked spare keeps its pre-write value, and the read-locked read does not return the stored value. An unlocked spare in the same OTP image is the negative control. The response code of a locked access is logged, not graded. Real fuse sense at `LC_PROD`. |YES for the claimed part; the evidence cell names the rest
|Token comparator redundancy fault delivery to the CPU |<<sep_efuse_token_match_fault_pic_test,`sep_efuse_token_match_fault_pic_test`>> |all, cpu |None |EL2 claims PIC source 40, reads the sticky `TOKEN_MATCH_FAULT` bit, and masks `meie[40]`; because the fault bits are software-read-only and clear only on reset, the check requires the source to be still requesting across the quiet window, which is what makes the mask the reason for the quiet. `RMA token match -> LC update` grades the comparator matrix on `sep_efuse_rma_token_rand_test` and is not re-credited here. |YES
|===

=== Scenario-Expanded Test Summary

One table per area, in the order of the Test Procedures. *Phase* is the scope
tier of the Verification Strategy. A Phase 3 row that reads "3, parked" names
a parked test (<<sep-vplan-phase3-parked>>). Each test name links to its entry. The
SV-UVM realization of a scenario keeps the cocotb name (<<sv-uvm-smoke>>).

==== Smoke

[cols="3,1,3,3,6",options="header"]

|Test Case |Phase |Run mode |VIP / Model |Description
|<<sep_axi_smoke_test,`sep_axi_smoke_test`>>
|1
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|Proves the SEP register fabric decodes and responds on the CPU LSU bus with no CPU booted.

|The minimum proof that the CPU boot path works end to end: firmware is compiled for the VeeR EL2 core, loaded into tightly-coupled memory, and the released core fetches, executes and prints over the firmware console.

The SRAM read latency is measured and logged, not graded.

[[sep-vplan-summary-1]]
==== Crypto and entropy

[cols="3,1,3,3,6",options="header"]
|===
|Test Case |Phase |Run mode |VIP / Model |Description
|<<sep_drbg_real_sink_multi_km_aes_test,`sep_drbg_real_sink_multi_km_aes_test`>>
|1
|no_cpu, real fuse sense
|ocah_axi_vip, entropy golden
|Proves that one entropy source feeds the Key Manager and AES at once: both draw beats inside one window, each AES beat equals the next word on its own feed, and the AES first block equals its golden.
|<<sep_esrc_e2e_smoke_test,`sep_esrc_e2e_smoke_test`>>
|1
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip, entropy golden
|Proves the entropy stack is alive end to end on the open-source build and that its output reaches a real consumer.
|<<sep_aes_mode_keysize_rand_test,`sep_aes_mode_keysize_rand_test`>>
|2
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense, +esrc_noise_force
|ocah_axi_vip
|Walks the nine-cell product of {ECB, CBC, CTR} x {128, 192, 256} in one invocation, with key, IV and plaintext randomised per cell.
|<<sep_hmac_sha_variant_rand_test,`sep_hmac_sha_variant_rand_test`>>
|2
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|Walks 17 cells: keyed HMAC over every legal key length for each of SHA-256/384/512, plus plain SHA for each variant.
|<<sep_kmac_mode_strength_rand_test,`sep_kmac_mode_strength_rand_test`>>
|2
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense, +esrc_noise_force
|ocah_axi_vip
|Thirteen cells across SHA3-224/256/384/512, SHAKE-128/256, cSHAKE-128/256 and KMAC-128/256 spanning all five key lengths.
|<<sep_crypto_edn_multisink_arbitration_test,`sep_crypto_edn_multisink_arbitration_test`>>
|2
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip, entropy golden
|Two crypto sinks draw EDN entropy concurrently and both complete correctly: each AES fork block matches its golden bit-exact and the KMAC digest matches its own, while both are shown to have taken real post-adapter EDN beats inside the contention window. An AES-alone baseline in which KMAC takes zero beats is the control that keeps the both-beats check from passing vacuously.
|<<sep_crypto_edn_round_robin_grant_test,`sep_crypto_edn_round_robin_grant_test`>>
|2
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip, entropy golden
|Holds AES and OTBN URND `edn_req` together, then grades that both clients are granted and that a prefix of post-adapter grants alternates. Round-robin order under simultaneous request is not claimed: a fixed-priority arbiter is not excluded. Then drives OTBN RND with a program blocked on the RND CSR and KMAC with a keyed digest, so all four adapter clients are live, value-checked consumers in one run, with the entropy pool scored alongside them as a fifth endpoint.
|<<sep_drbg_gen_segmentation_test,`sep_drbg_gen_segmentation_test`>>
|2
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip, entropy golden
|Phase 2 DRBG iconic feature: a CSRNG Generate command completes at the commanded length and `gen_last` asserts. The trailing CTR_DRBG Update is not observed on this vehicle.
|<<sep_crypto_per_ip_reset_isolation_test,`sep_crypto_per_ip_reset_isolation_test`>>
|2
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|Proves the per-IP software reset domains are isolated, using live crypto results rather than a poked status bit: each engine computes a golden-checked value, then one engine is reset and the other's held result must survive bit-exact.
|<<sep_trng_reset_recovery_test,`sep_trng_reset_recovery_test`>>
|2
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense, +esrc_noise_force
|ocah_axi_vip, entropy golden
|The counterpart for the one reset shared by ESRC, CSRNG and EDN: the pool is filled from the internal chain, every consumer is held, and the reset must wait for all three converted AXI-Lite paths to isolate before it asserts. Proves the downstream clear crosses into `clk_i` through two synchronizer stages, the pool scrubs its stale and half-packed data, the isolated paths answer SLVERR while the external source-select register stays outside the domain, and recovery is fresh-only and ordered — consumers are released only after fresh pool progress.
|<<sep_abr_mldsa_keygen_kat_test,`sep_abr_mldsa_keygen_kat_test`>>
|2
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|ML-DSA-87 key generation through the ABR register aperture: reset status, an ACVP keyGen known-answer public key, completion status, the PIC error and notification legs, `ZEROIZE`, and single-bit seed sensitivity.
|<<sep_abr_mldsa_sign_verify_kat_test,`sep_abr_mldsa_sign_verify_kat_test`>>
|2
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|ML-DSA-87 signing and verification through the same aperture: a deterministic external-mu signature compared word-for-word against the ACVP sigGen vector, and the sigVer pair that must verify and must not, with `MLDSA_VERIFY_RES` compared against each signature's c~.
|<<sep_abr_pipelined_read_test,`sep_abr_pipelined_read_test`>>
|2
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|ABR is the one crypto block reached over AHB, through `axi_lite_to_ahb`. An SoC master may hold several single-beat reads outstanding with distinct `ARID`s, and each must be answered with its own register's value. Every read in the sweep targets the SAME read-only register, so every response must carry the identical value and no reordering or per-id confusion can explain a difference. A bridge that drops `HADDR[2:0]` on streamed reads answers zero with an OKAY response from the third read in flight.
|<<sep_abr_mlkem_kat_test,`sep_abr_mlkem_kat_test`>>
|2
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|ML-KEM-1024 key generation, encapsulation and decapsulation through the ML-KEM register block: the two keys, the ciphertext and the shared key each compared against the published ACVP vector, with decapsulation walked as both the valid case and the modified-ciphertext case that FIPS-203 answers by implicit rejection. Also the Adams Bridge `SW_RESET_N` domain: a live encaps result survives a sibling engine's reset bit-exact, the host path returns SLVERR while the ABR bit is held, and the result does not survive ABR's own reset.
|<<sep_abr_pipelined_read_matrix_test,`sep_abr_pipelined_read_matrix_test`>>
|2
|no_cpu + external SMN master, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|Several reads in flight on the ABR aperture each return the word they name: concurrent single-beat reads of `MLDSA_VERSION1` at depths 1, 2, 3, 4 and 8 on each TB master, the four ML-DSA identity words in several orders and on one ARID, and both masters at once. The AR and R handshakes recorded on the pins prove the reads overlapped, and every R beat is graded by the RID it carried.
|<<sep_abr_access_size_test,`sep_abr_access_size_test`>>
|2
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|The ABR access-size rules of `adams_bridge.adoc`: a 64-bit read returns both registers it covers, a narrow read the addressed bytes with every other byte lane zero, a 64-bit write lands exactly the words its strobe covers whole, and every partial-word strobe answers SLVERR without moving the register. Past the register map (`0xC018`) the crypto demux answers every word read, word write and partial-word write with DECERR, and no live register moves (`hw/sys/sep/doc/adams_bridge.adoc`, aperture-decode paragraph).
|<<sep_abr_concurrent_rw_test,`sep_abr_concurrent_rw_test`>>
|2
|no_cpu + external SMN master, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|Seeded interleaved reads and writes from both TB masters at once, several outstanding on each and both response channels throttled, graded by a scoreboard that bounds each read by its register owner's write window and checks each register's final value.
|<<sep_abr_absent_decerr_test,`sep_abr_absent_decerr_test`>>
|2
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense, `lsu_stub_abr_absent` model, Verilator only
|ocah_axi_vip, KM CPU image
|The build without Adams Bridge (`SEP_ABR_EN` not defined): host reads and writes of the ABR aperture answer DECERR, and KM CPU stores and loads of the ABR wrapper key aperture set AXI_DECERR, with HMAC controls on both paths answering clean.
|<<sep_entropy_pool_aperture_test,`sep_entropy_pool_aperture_test`>>
|2
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip, entropy golden
|Every seed walks the high-bit mirrors of the three live registers, refuses writes to each live offset and one unmapped offset, exercises all three AW/W arrival orders on the shared write path, and refuses a 32-bit read of each register's upper word without popping or disturbing the FIFO.
|<<sep_otbn_compute_rand_test,`sep_otbn_compute_rand_test`>>
|2
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|Loads a tiny RV32I program into OTBN IMEM, stages two seeded DMEM operands, issues EXECUTE, and checks `ERR_BITS==0` plus the DMEM result against an independent Python golden.
|<<sep_esrc_ht_watermark_arming_test,`sep_esrc_ht_watermark_arming_test`>>
|2
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip, entropy golden
|Health-test watermark arming: each supported selector arms to `0x0000` high or `0xFFFF` low on `MODULE_ENABLE` 0->1, a selector write leaves `HT_WATERMARK` at the entry value, and an illegal selector resolves to `REPCNT_HI`. Scope: the ESRC → DRBG → EDN datapath row owns CHK1–CHK4 and stays where it is.
|<<sep_esrc_fips_lock_test,`sep_esrc_fips_lock_test`>>
|2
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip, entropy golden
|The FIPS lock makes ESRC configuration write-once: a pre-lock write moves the field off reset, the same field rejects a poke after `LOCK=1`, write-0 cannot clear the lock, and `rst_ni` is the only way back. The lock also freezes the debug-pin mux and both observe-tap enables: one tap held at 1 rejects a clear, the other held at 0 rejects a set, while `NOISE_OBS_CTRL.LANE_SEL` stays writable. `FIFO_CTRL.ENABLE`, cleared before the lock, rejects a set under it, so software cannot re-open the `FIFO_RDATA` read path of the DRBG seed words. The advisory RCT/APT cutoffs derived from `MIN_ENTROPY_H` are graded against an SP 800-90B oracle. Scope: health-test *quality* is not claimed.
|<<sep_esrc_e2e_fifo_closed_test,`sep_esrc_e2e_fifo_closed_test`>>
|2
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense, +esrc_noise_force, +esrc_fifo_closed
|ocah_axi_vip, entropy golden
|`FIFO_CTRL.ENABLE=0` closes the `FIFO_RDATA` read path but not the DRBG seed path. The seed and genbits still match the golden, `FIFO_STATUS.LEVEL` and `WPTR` stay at reset while seed words cross, `FIFO_RDATA` pops of the empty FIFO set `FIFO_UNDERFLOW`, and under `FIPS_LOCK` a write of ENABLE=1 is refused.

[[sep-vplan-summary-2]]
==== Fabric and remap

[cols="3,1,3,3,6",options="header"]
|===
|Test Case |Phase |Run mode |VIP / Model |Description
|<<sep_address_map_test,`sep_address_map_test`>>
|1
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|Prove the CPU-LSU decodes `sep_cpu_ctrl` and one safe CSR in every LSU-reachable CSR block (including Adams Bridge and the entropy pool).
|<<sep_cpu_ifu_lsu_alias_remap_matrix_test,`sep_cpu_ifu_lsu_alias_remap_matrix_test`>>
|1
|cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip, CPU FW
|Proves the CPU-side address alias window remaps both data and instruction accesses to physical SRAM.
|<<sep_fabric_inbound_filter_rule_matrix_test,`sep_fabric_inbound_filter_rule_matrix_test`>>
|2
|no_cpu, real PROD fuse sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|Brings the lifecycle controller up in PROD so the inbound filter is genuinely active (`sep_debug=0`, value-checked against the lifecycle golden), then walks entries 0 and 7, two address windows, and `{rw, read-only, write-only}` at `src_id=0` (match-all), plus entry 0 / window 0 × `{rw, r, w}` at `src_id=0x5` with a matching AXI `user[3:0]` and one src-mismatch cell — 16 cells in one invocation — then the same-page `allow_burst=1` granule, page-bound, and `FILTER_CONFIG.locked` on entry 15. `CHK-CONFIG-LOCK` requires both writes to the locked `FILTER_CONFIG` to complete DECERR, and the frozen `allow_burst` to still pass a 2-beat read inside the widened page.
|<<sep_fabric_remap_filter_csr_bank_test,`sep_fabric_remap_filter_csr_bank_test`>>
|2
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|R/W, 64-bit, RO, valid, and write-once-set across the alias/AP/STEE remap and inbound/outbound filter CSR banks, plus per-entry storage independence across all 64 filter and alias entries. AP and STEE are checked on one seeded region each per seed.
|<<sep_fabric_slot_csr_strobe_rand_test,`sep_fabric_slot_csr_strobe_rand_test`>>
|2
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|Every one of the 96 fabric remap and filter slots, graded against a register model built from the generated IP-XACT field metadata: RDL reset values; 64-, 32-, 16- and 8-bit writes, with seeded data on every inactive byte lane, land on exactly the strobed lanes of writable fields, and the fill reaches each slot's register block; a write and a read of each slot outstanding together on `s_axi`, with the response channels held and with W ahead of AW; every filter entry locked, after which writes chosen to move it leave it unchanged.
|<<sep_fabric_output_remap_datapath_proxy_test,`sep_fabric_output_remap_datapath_proxy_test`>>
|2
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|Proves that a programmed output-remap window translates a live beat to its remapped target, and that the outbound filter answers DECERR for a beat outside the remapped allow set and for the same beat with its allow entry disabled.
|<<sep_fabric_outbound_remap_filter_walk_test,`sep_fabric_outbound_remap_filter_walk_test`>>
|2
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|Every AP/STEE output-remap region, each paired with its own outbound-filter entry, at full 56-bit width: offsets O and ~O, windows holding, above, below and exactly at the remapped beat, and valid clear. The block-by-default filter grades the remapped address on every live beat.
|<<sep_fabric_inbound_filter_window_walk_test,`sep_fabric_inbound_filter_window_walk_test`>>
|2
|no_cpu, real PROD fuse sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|Every inbound-filter entry at full 56-bit START/END width, on live external reads of a staged CSR: inside the window OKAY with the value, outside DECERR without it.
|<<sep_reg_bit_bash_rand_test,`sep_reg_bit_bash_rand_test`>>
|2
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|Proves that every register in the generated SystemRDL export reads its RDL reset value, that a write moves exactly its software-usable bits, that read-only and reserved bits hold, and that every side-effect-free register stores a seeded value.
|<<sep_fabric_deadspace_decode_test,`sep_fabric_deadspace_decode_test`>>
|2
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|Negative control on intra-block dead space. Each block owns an address window and populates only part of it; an access past the allocated size owns no register and must be refused. Window ends come from `hw/sys/sep/doc/memory_map.adoc`, which states the rule: the fabric refuses an address past the extent a unit allocates, and such an access never reaches a unit. Landing on a live register instead is a hard failure. Bursts are graded in the crypto region only: `hw/sys/sep/doc/crypto.adoc` ("Single-Beat Access Only") answers every beat of a burst there DECERR, and no beat reaches an accelerator. A burst into any other register region is outside the specification (`hw/sys/sep/doc/memory_map.adoc`, "Single-Beat Register Access") and is not graded. The TRNG window, forwarded whole to an adopter endpoint and terminated in the reference integration, must refuse every access, alias no neighbouring ESRC register, and answer the code `memory_map.adoc` states for a window with no external TRNG: DECERR on a read and on a write.
|<<sep_fabric_local_alias_datapath_test,`sep_fabric_local_alias_datapath_test`>>
|2
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|Scope is the sixteen programmable local-master regions ahead of the system-peripherals routing demux (`hw/sys/sep/doc/fabric.adoc`, "Address Remapping").
|<<sep_fabric_local_alias_offset_walk_test,`sep_fabric_local_alias_offset_walk_test`>>
|2
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|Every local-master alias region at full 56-bit START/END/offset width. A hit sends the CPU-LSU beat to a 56-bit SMU-path target that one outbound-filter granule admits; a miss or valid clear returns the staged local word.
|<<sep_axi_order_sweep_test,`sep_axi_order_sweep_test`>>
|2
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|AW and W are independent channels with no ordering requirement between them (IHI 0022 A3.3), so AW-first, W-first and same-cycle are all legal and each must deliver the write without displacing its neighbour. The orderings are presented at the s_axi fabric ingress and graded end to end at every write-safe register: 321 cells over 27 blocks, crossed with 1/2/4-byte sizes on the scratch words. The crossbar can re-serialize a write before it reaches a block adapter, so no per-adapter ordering is claimed. The ordering each cell presented is read off the AW/W valid assertions and asserted against what was requested.
|<<sep_axi_order_sweep_m_axi_test,`sep_axi_order_sweep_m_axi_test`>>
|2
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|The same ordering walk driven from the SMN inbound master. m_axi reaches the same eleven IP blocks through the inbound filter, which denies by default, so the walk opens allow windows from the CPU-LSU side first. The sixteen `INBOUND_FILTER_CTRL` blocks are excluded and counted: those CSRs gate the access in flight, so writing them mid-walk would measure filter reprogramming rather than adapter ordering. Unlike the CPU-LSU splice, this path grants a W-first handshake.
|<<sep_drbg_axil_adapter_port_arbitration_test,`sep_drbg_axil_adapter_port_arbitration_test`>>
|2
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|TB-instantiated `drbg_axil64_lane_adapter`
|All three legal AW/W/AR orderings at a `drbg_axil64_lane_adapter` port. Write address, write data, and read address are independent channels (IHI 0022 A3.3). The adapter accepts each write half independently until that half is pending, and accepts a read only when neither write half is pending; both accesses must retire under every ordering. The DUT's own adapters cannot be presented every ordering from the fabric -- the crossbar delivers W one cycle after AW and re-serializes to that order whatever the master does -- so this leaf drives a TB-owned second instance of the same module at its port, with its own reset so cells are independent. A lone write and a lone read are required to retire first. Grades the MODULE; the two leaves below grade the SEP integration.
|<<sep_drbg_axil_adapter_refusal_test,`sep_drbg_axil_adapter_refusal_test`>>
|2
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip; TB-instantiated `drbg_axil64_lane_adapter`
|An unsupported write at a `drbg_axil64_lane_adapter` -- a strobe that is not `0x0F` or `0xF0`, or an address that is not 4-byte aligned -- answers SLVERR and forwards nothing, and the next supported access completes normally. On the DUT's CSRNG and EDN adapters, reached over s_axi, "nothing forwarded" is graded on each adapter's AXI-Lite-32 request probe, by the target register keeping its value and by the lane's `PERIPH_BUS_ERR_STATUS` bit staying clear; a supported write of the same value is required to be seen on the probe and to land. The TB-owned vehicle covers the strobe patterns one AXI transfer cannot place at a DUT port and observes its downstream side directly. Misaligned reads, wrong-lane strobes and the other lane of a read are driven and logged but not graded: the specification states no rule for them.
|<<sep_drbg_axil_concurrent_csrng_aw_then_ar_test,`sep_drbg_axil_concurrent_csrng_aw_then_ar_test`>>
|2
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|The same arbitration contract on the DUT's own CSRNG lane adapter, reached as ordinary CSR traffic over the SEP fabric. AR is presented while a write is half committed. Calibration measures where each channel arrives at the adapter port, so the overlap is placed from measured latency rather than guessed. Overlap is confirmed at the adapter's own port, not at the TB master.
|<<sep_drbg_axil_concurrent_edn_aw_then_ar_test,`sep_drbg_axil_concurrent_edn_aw_then_ar_test`>>
|2
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|The same contract on the second DUT lane adapter, the EDN control path. `drbg.sv` instantiates `drbg_axil64_lane_adapter` twice, so a defect in one instance is not evidence about the other.
|<<sep_axi_id_routing_test,`sep_axi_id_routing_test`>>
|2
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|AXI transaction IDs on the response path. The crossbars prepend the master index to the incoming `AxID` and the demux keeps one outstanding counter per ID, so the ID is address-like state and a master matches its responses by it. Eight scratch words hold eight distinct values and eight reads are held outstanding together, one per ID; each must return its own word, and the top ID must not alias a lower slot. This leaf grades response-by-ID on the local fabric scratch words.
|<<sep_axi_strobe_window_test,`sep_axi_strobe_window_test`>>
|2
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|Narrow-write lane masking against the AXI strobe rule (IHI 0022 A3.4.3), plus write/readback of the OTBN IMEM and DMEM memory windows. The KMAC state window is not probed: it is gated by the engine's own configuration, so a probe there would test KMAC bring-up rather than the read mask. A window behind an SRAM-style adapter applies the mask on reads, unlike a register behind a register adapter, so a bridge driving that mask from the write strobe returns zeros with a correct response — only a data compare sees it.
|<<sep_axi_partial_lane_read_test,`sep_axi_partial_lane_read_test`>>
|2
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|Read-side counterpart of the strobe test: a read narrower than the 64-bit beat only obligates the responder to drive the addressed byte lanes (a 32-bit CSR leaves RDATA[63:32] undriven; a narrow-ARSIZE beat leaves every non-addressed lane undriven), so the value handed to the test must be a pure function of the addressed lanes. Every read is compared against an independent golden and repeated around a checked disturbing access, so a result that leaks undriven lanes or leftover channel state fails as a mismatch.
|<<sep_crypto_csr_wide_access_test,`sep_crypto_csr_wide_access_test`>>
|2
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|`hw/sys/sep/doc/crypto.adoc` (the `sep_crypto_axi_interconnect` paragraph) decodes one 64-bit AXI slave port to the crypto host paths (OTBN, HMAC, AES, KMAC, ESRC, CSRNG, EDN), with data-width conversion where the endpoint requires it. The test walks every host path the spec names. At OTBN, HMAC, AES, KMAC and ESRC a 64-bit read must equal the two 32-bit reads of the same words. At CSRNG and EDN, `hw/ip/drbg/doc/architecture.adoc` ("Bus Protocol Adaptation") narrows a 64-bit access to the 32-bit lane selected by address bit [2], so an aligned 64-bit read must carry the addressed register in bits [31:0]. A representative floor requires at least two apertures with distinct non-zero halves, where zeroing, duplication and swapping are all observable; pairs with a zero or equal half claim exact equality only. The same reads are then held concurrently with distinct `ARID`s, at distinct addresses and at every aperture: serial reads are answered by an otherwise idle path, and only concurrency can show one read being answered with another's data. The depth is DV-owned, not a hardware slot count. No burst is involved: the "Single-Beat Access Only" section of `hw/sys/sep/doc/crypto.adoc` answers any `AxLEN != 0` crypto access DECERR.
|<<sep_axi_map_refuse_test,`sep_axi_map_refuse_test`>>
|2
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|Inter-block reserved space: reserved rows in the DV-owned table in `env/sep_axi_decode_map.py` allocate nothing, so an access there must not answer OKAY. The refusal expectation comes from that table. Named reserved spans are excluded from the probe set with a counted reason. The walk grades refusal only: it accepts any refusal and logs the code. The code `sep.rdl` states (`ocah_resp`) is graded by `sep_unmapped_access_policy_test` at the points that test probes. Walks the gaps BETWEEN block windows; `sep_fabric_deadspace_decode_test` walks the dead tail INSIDE one.
|<<sep_unmapped_access_policy_test,`sep_unmapped_access_policy_test`>>
|2
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|Unmapped points next to live registers that no other leaf probes: the system-CSR holes, the reserved row above SEP CPU control, the upper half of the mailbox page, the space past each eFuse sibling block and past the SPI host, and the tail of every remap and filter array slot. The SEP components table of the generated memory map states, per unit, the response code and read data at each such point; every probe is graded against it and must never alias. Every array slot tail inside base + `SEP_TOP_<ARRAY>_TOTAL_SIZE`, the RDL allocation, the last slot's tail included, must read zero with OKAY. Every probe is checked against distinct values programmed into the neighbouring live words; a live word whose RDL field has no reset is written before it is read.
|<<sep_fabric_inbound_rebase_test,`sep_fabric_inbound_rebase_test`>>
|3
|no_cpu, real PROD fuse sense
|System Interface master, LSU splice, SRAM and scratch read-back model
|Proves that an inbound request meets the inbound filter on the global address first, that the window at `SEP_GLOBAL_BASE_ADDR` rebases to address 0, and that the peripheral crossbar answers DECERR at or above `0x4000_0000`. Also proves that the units which the inbound port does not connect answer DECERR at a rebased aperture. Extends the open inbound rule-matrix test and the open lifecycle gating test with the rebase cells only.

|<<sep_fabric_filter_match_priority_rand_test,`sep_fabric_filter_match_priority_rand_test`>>
|3
|no_cpu, real PROD fuse sense
|System Interface and LSU masters, filter reference model written from the filter document
|Proves that an entry matches only on enabled, range, `prot[1]`, source ID and burst, that the lowest match wins, that a failed match falls through, and that no match blocks (the inbound single-attribute cells and the inbound reset-state deny that an open test grades run as controls). Also proves the outbound default deny after reset, the default deny of both instances while armed but disabled, and that the 8-byte and 4 KB granules widen a range as stated.

|<<sep_fabric_outbound_route_attr_test,`sep_fabric_outbound_route_attr_test`>>
|3
|no_cpu, real PROD fuse sense, then DEMOTE_1
|LSU and System Interface masters, outbound responder, filter reference model
|Proves that the route demux sends the SMU aperture and the AP and STEE regions to the outbound port and that the AP and STEE remaps force the source ID to OTHERS while the LSU drives another value. Proves that local scratch and `SEP_SW_DEBUG` accesses stay off that port and that the outbound filter has no bypass. Extends the open outbound remap tests and the open lifecycle tests with the write cells and the no-bypass pair only.

|<<sep_fabric_alias_remap_attr_rand_test,`sep_fabric_alias_remap_attr_rand_test`>>
|3
|no_cpu, `+skip_fuse_sense`
|LSU master, outbound responder, alias-remap reference model
|Proves that an alias-remap hit is `start_addr <= address < end_addr` and replaces all four AxCACHE bits, and that a miss, and a region with `valid` 0, keeps address and AxCACHE. A hit lands at or above `0x1_0000_0000`, so the hit cells also prove the route of that address class.

|<<sep_fabric_smc_route_test,`sep_fabric_smc_route_test`>>
|3
|no_cpu, `+skip_fuse_sense`, SMC aperture drawn per seed
|LSU master, SMC aperture drive and SMC responder (BH-SMC, added hook), SMC request probe (observation tap)
|Proves that the first and last word of the SMC aperture, as the SMC supplies it, match first in the route order and go to the SMC port, and that the word above the aperture top does not reach the SMC port. The `rom_fw` boot tests grade the basic route and the unfiltered path.

|<<sep_fabric_extension_port_window_test,`sep_fabric_extension_port_window_test`>>
|3
|no_cpu, real PROD fuse sense
|LSU and System Interface masters, extension port probe (observation tap)
|Proves that the extension port serves `0x2000_0000` to `0x3FFF_FFFF` except the eFuse shim window, for LSU and System Interface, and that a filter-denied burst never reaches it.

|<<sep_fabric_row_response_matrix_test,`sep_fabric_row_response_matrix_test`>>
|3
|no_cpu, real PROD fuse sense
|System Interface and LSU masters, generated memory-map table model
|Proves that the memory-map rows answer their Reserved, Past Extent, Hole in Extent and live-word cells with the stated code and data, that no probe aliases onto a live register, and that an inbound burst to the crypto region answers DECERR on every beat.

|<<sep_cpu_lsu_alias_window_twin_test,`sep_cpu_lsu_alias_window_twin_test`>>
|3
|cpu, `+skip_fuse_sense`
|CPU LSU firmware master, direct-address twin model
|Proves that an address in the 768 MiB local alias window answers as its direct twin on the scratch, AES, system CSR, SPI host, entropy source and SRAM targets. Extends the open alias matrix test with the unit classes and the window edges only.

|<<sep_fabric_dma_endpoint_matrix_test,`sep_fabric_dma_endpoint_matrix_test`>>
|3
|cpu, `+skip_fuse_sense`, SMC aperture plusargs
|DMA firmware master, outbound filter source-ID model, request probes (observation taps), SMC aperture plusargs and SMC memory model (BH-SMC, added hook)
|Proves that the DMA reaches the scratch, WDT, AES register, register and outbound targets, does not reach its own CSR or the boot ROM, and leaves AxUSER at zero in the outbound source-ID stack. The SMC leg runs on every seed through BH-SMC, a top-port drive. Extends the open DMA tests and the Phase 3 DMA transfer and TCM tests with the remaining endpoint pairs only.
|===

The alert index leg samples `crypto_alert_pulse_probe_o`, an observation tap the test adds to the SEP_TB_ARCH observation-probe list. The boundary input leg drives `sep_ext_interrupts_i`, a top-port drive the test adds to `docs/SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc`.

|Proves the enable gate on PIC sources 9, 10 and 11, STATUS.ERROR, SIZE_ERROR and INTR_STATE.DMA_ERROR for a zero size and for hashing at width 1 or 2, and that the engine takes a legal copy after a size error with no reset. The range lock check is Blocked: the specification does not name the write that applies the lock.
|<<sep_dma_abort_recovery_rand_test,`sep_dma_abort_recovery_rand_test`>>
|3
|no_cpu, real fuse sense
|ocah_axi_vip, `dma_busy_probe_o` (approved); `dma_axi_req_probe_o`, `dma_status_probe_o` (observation taps)
|Proves that CONTROL.ABORT during a transfer sets ABORTED, that no beat follows the ABORTED rise before the recovery GO write, and that the engine copies correctly after the abort with no reset.
|<<sep_spi_dma_handshake_rand_test,`sep_spi_dma_handshake_rand_test`>>
|3
|no_cpu, real fuse sense
|ocah_axi_vip, ocah_spi_vip, `spi_lsio_trigger_probe_o`, `dma_busy_probe_o`, `spi_tx_qd_probe_o`, `sep_internal_interrupts_probe_o` (approved)
|Proves that the DMA moves no word while mask bit 0 is clear, keeps GO set, raises no CHUNK_DONE, and stops when firmware clears GO. The RX word order check is Blocked: the specification does not name the Rx line.
|===

Also relevant, owned by <<phase2-mailbox-irq-pic,Mailbox, IRQ, PIC>>: <<sep_spi_ot_host_csr_irq_rand_test>> covers the SPI host CSR and interrupt surface. It is graded there so each test has one owning bucket.

|<<sep_sec_dis_override_test,`sep_sec_dis_override_test`>>
|2
|no_cpu, real fuse sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|A match is reachable because `tb_top` binds `SEP_SEC_DISABLE_TOKEN` to SHA-256 of the all-zero token. `CHK-OVR-ALONE` requires the `sep_reset_n` override, before any match, to release the sense-gated reset and leave feature control closed. `CHK-SENSE-GATED-RESET` requires that the match leaves the reset held. `CHK-FEAT-PRESENSE` requires that the match opens feature control before sensing finishes. `CHK-OVR-AFTER` requires the same override inputs to release and re-hold the reset after the match. `CHK-MAP-OPEN` requires an OKAY `LC_STATE` shadow-map read in that window. `CHK-SENSE-STUCK` requires that the match stops the sense. After a `rst_ni` pulse with the token kept, the `CHK-RETAIN-*` checkers require SEC_DIS to stay active, sense to stay stopped, the map read to return OKAY and the override to release the reset. `CHK-DROP` then clears SEC_DIS, and `CHK-SENSE-CONTROL` requires a full sense after a second pulse to finish inside the stuck window.
|<<sep_sec_dis_reset_reach_test,`sep_sec_dis_reset_reach_test`>>
|2
|no_cpu, real fuse sense
|ocah_axi_vip, `jtag_ic_reset_reg`

|<<sep_efuse_illegal_state_fail_closed_test,`sep_efuse_illegal_state_fail_closed_test`>>
|2
|no_cpu, real fuse sense
|DUT probes and a one-cycle state force
|The eFuse read and program interfaces fail closed from an illegal state encoding, and the read-error flag is set by a failed or timed-out read and cleared by the next legal request.

One seed-selected field carries its eFuse read lock, which withholds that field's shadow read and leaves both KM views intact. The KM-side `OTP_READ_LOCK` then zeroes the KMCSR words and leaves the input intact.
|<<sep_efuse_window_decode_reset_test,`sep_efuse_window_decode_reset_test`>>
|3
|no_cpu, real fuse sense

|3
|no_cpu, real fuse sense
|Frontdoor master, sensed-shadow probe, OTP preload, `jtag_id_i` drive (added hook)
|Proves that the used bits of nine narrow shadow registers (the ten 32-bit registers except `LOCKS_SPARE`) read back as staged at edge values that the seed draws, and that a drawn non-zero `jtag_id_i` enters no shadow word. The offset and width of all 43 shadow registers run as a logged control.

|<<sep_lcc_state_codeword_rand_test,`sep_lcc_state_codeword_rand_test`>>
|3
|no_cpu, real fuse sense
|`lc_sigint_inject_i`, `lcc_feat_ctrl_probe_o`, JTAG master, JTAG lifecycle filter inject (added hook)
|Proves that a non-complementary byte at the lifecycle decoder input raises `lc_sigint_err_o` and forces feature control to 0 in each open-profile nibble, unless SEC_DIS forces all ones, and, in a TEST_DEV leaf, that the JTAG port admits only the token block under an integrity error at the JTAG lifecycle filter.

|<<sep_lcc_feat_ctrl_state_matrix_rand_test,`sep_lcc_feat_ctrl_state_matrix_rand_test`>>
|3
|no_cpu, real fuse sense
|Frontdoor master, `lcc_feat_ctrl_probe_o`, demotion output and DFT disable port probes, OTP preload, OTP bank probe and `efuse_shim_command_req_o` tap (observation taps)
|Proves that feature control equals the profile row for all 16 raw state codes, demotion and SEC_DIS cells with random disable vectors, that each disable bit closes its own feature bit and software can only add restrictions, that the two demotion registers follow their lock and write rules over random operation sequences and cold reset, and that the two fuse DFT disable ports split the 32 combinations of the five named disable bits by their case conjunction.

|<<sep_efuse_transient_rma_rand_test,`sep_efuse_transient_rma_rand_test`>>
|3
|no_cpu, real fuse sense
|Frontdoor master, sensed-shadow probe, OTP preload, direct OTP read, `efuse_shim_command_req_o` tap (observation tap)
|Proves that with the transient enable at 1 a token match updates the state shadow without a bus write, that with it at 0 a match changes nothing, that cold reset restores the sensed state, and that a program of `LC_STATE` bit 1 or bit 2 lands only when the channel that gates the bit is matched.

|<<sep_efuse_token_channel_rand_test,`sep_efuse_token_channel_rand_test`>>
|3
|no_cpu, real fuse sense, VCS only
|Frontdoor master, token word tap (observation tap), OTP preload
|Proves that each of three token channels hashes its eight-word array on its own go bit, returns only Idle, Match or Mismatch, holds its result, stays independent of the other channels, and gives its result again after a cold reset once sensing is done.

|<<sep_efuse_token_fault_matrix_test,`sep_efuse_token_fault_matrix_test`>>
|3
|no_cpu, real fuse sense
|`token_cmp_fault_inject_i`, digest latch probes and comparator rail taps (observation taps), comparator copy select and test-enable inject (added hooks), PIC source 40 probe
|Proves that a collapsed pair or a disagreeing comparator copy forces the error code, sets that channel's sticky flag and the PIC request, that a common-mode flip on RMA_CHIPLET inverts the held result and raises nothing, and that a test enable freezes the retained digest and valid bit of every channel.

|<<sep_efuse_interface_status_rand_test,`sep_efuse_interface_status_rand_test`>>
|3
|no_cpu, real fuse sense
|Frontdoor master, OTP preload, read and program interface outputs, OTP bank probe and `efuse_shim_command_req_o` tap (observation taps)
|Proves that each illegal read or program request sets only its own status flag and that each flag clears as described, and that a program with data 0 and a program before sense change no bank bit.

|<<sep_efuse_lock_slot_matrix_rand_test,`sep_efuse_lock_slot_matrix_rand_test`>>
|3
|no_cpu, real fuse sense
|Frontdoor master, sensed-shadow probe, OTP preload, OTP bank probe and `efuse_shim_command_req_o` tap (observation taps)
|Proves that every lock slot places its write-lock and read-lock bits at the described positions, locks only its own field, does not block the sensing state machine, that `LOCKS`, `LOCKS_SPARE` and `LC_STATE` stay readable with every assigned lock bit burned, and that a programmed bit reaches the shadow only at the next sense, with the OR of all rounds.

|<<sep_efuse_secure_tm_matrix_rand_test,`sep_efuse_secure_tm_matrix_rand_test`>>
|3
|no_cpu, real fuse sense
|Frontdoor master, JTAG master, `secure_tm_req_i`, sensed-shadow probe, direct OTP read, `efuse_shim_command_req_o` tap (observation tap)
|Proves that with secure test mode active no program changes the bank, the four secret fields read zero at the sensed-shadow output, and the protection shadows ignore software and JTAG writes, with the feature control as a control.

|<<sep_efuse_jtag_access_rand_test,`sep_efuse_jtag_access_rand_test`>>
|3
|no_cpu, real fuse sense
|JTAG master, frontdoor master, direct OTP read, `efuse_shim_command_req_o` tap (observation tap)
|Proves that in PROD and in RMA_SIP the JTAG port reaches only the token block, that every other access completes with an error and changes nothing, and that no feature-control bit gates the port.

|<<sep_sec_dis_timing_rand_test,`sep_sec_dis_timing_rand_test`>>
|3
|no_cpu, real fuse sense, VCS only
|Frontdoor master, `security_disable_o`, `sep_fuse_sense_done_o`, sensed-shadow probe, IC_RESET TDR driver
|Proves that a SEC_DIS match made before sensing keeps sense done low and the sensed shadow words at their reset values, that a match made during sensing keeps sense done low and the sensed shadow words unchanged, and that every shadow word reads its reset value after a cold reset under the match, with the sense stop, the open map read and the recovery as controls.

|<<sep_efuse_shim_response_fault_test,`sep_efuse_shim_response_fault_test`>>
|3
|no_cpu, real fuse sense, injected shim response
|Sense status inject and shim response hold (added hooks), read and program interface outputs
|Proves that a sense response with its status bit set leaves its shadow word at the reset value and still lets sense done rise, and that a program request shows busy and not done while its response is withheld and done with no error after it.
|===

Also relevant, owned by <<phase2-mailbox-irq-pic,Mailbox, IRQ, PIC>>: <<sep_locked_field_access_irq_path_test>> and <<sep_efuse_token_match_fault_pic_test>> reach eFuse state, but their contract is the IRQ/PIC delivery path. They are graded there so each test has one owning bucket.

[[sep-vplan-summary-7]]
==== Key Manager vault

[cols="3,1,3,3,6",options="header"]
|===
|Test Case |Phase |Run mode |VIP / Model |Description
|<<sep_km_otbn_sideload_kat_test,`sep_km_otbn_sideload_kat_test`>>
|1
|no_cpu, real fuse sense
|ocah_axi_vip, KM path / golden
|Proves the full key-distribution chain from the real entropy datapath to a real cryptographic consumer: the OpenTitan entropy stack seeds the Key Manager, the Key Manager's firmware accepts a known 384-bit key over its mailbox and sideloads it to OTBN over the dedicated key bus, and an OTBN program running on the real core reconstructs the key from its two shares and writes it to data memory, where the host reads it back and compares against the known value.
|<<sep_km_aes_sideload_kat_test,`sep_km_aes_sideload_kat_test`>>
|1
|no_cpu, real fuse sense
|ocah_axi_vip, KM path / golden
|Proves that a known key loaded over the Key Manager mailbox and sideloaded to AES encrypts to the independent golden and decrypts back to the plaintext, and that the software-key path reaches the same golden.
|<<sep_km_hmac_sideload_kat_test,`sep_km_hmac_sideload_kat_test`>>
|1
|no_cpu, real fuse sense
|ocah_axi_vip, KM path / golden
|Proves that a known key loaded over the Key Manager mailbox and sideloaded to HMAC produces the independent HMAC-SHA256 golden, and that the digest differs from the golden of the decoy software key.
|<<sep_km_kmac_sideload_kat_test,`sep_km_kmac_sideload_kat_test`>>
|1
|no_cpu, real fuse sense
|ocah_axi_vip, KM path / golden

|<<sep_km_isolate_termination_test,`sep_km_isolate_termination_test`>>
|2
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense

Every other isolation entry drives the SEP host port and so reaches the `host_*` paths only; the KM masters the `km_*` paths, so a KM-CPU image is the vehicle. The same store to the HMAC key aperture is clean, then raises an AXI error while `km_hmac` is isolated, then is clean again.
|<<sep_km_ic_reset_release_test,`sep_km_ic_reset_release_test`>>

RANDCFG extra transfer dest (HMAC / KMAC / AES / OTBN) writes one classic `cp_dest` cell per seed. The four sideload KATs are the MUST-fill owners for HMAC/KMAC/AES/OTBN consume.
