|<<sep_km_otp_interface_rand_test,`sep_km_otp_interface_rand_test`>>
|3
|no_cpu, POKE, real fuse sense, HK-OTP
|KM bus operation image with result words, secure test mode and fuse sense probes, host mailbox master and host life-cycle path, `HK-OTP` bit flip inject
|Proves that the OTP readout equals the staged fuse image in dual-rail form, that it reads zero under the read locks and in secure test mode, that a single-rail flip sets OTP_SIGINT, and that a two-rail change sets OTP_CHANGE.

|<<sep_km_key_bank_register_rand_test,`sep_km_key_bank_register_rand_test`>>
|3
|no_cpu, POKE, +skip_fuse_sense
|KM bus operation image with result words, host mailbox master
|Proves that key-share registers read zero, that key_valid follows its set and clear rules, and that the ABR shared-key valid bit is set only by hardware and gates its interrupt.

|<<sep_km_sram_scrambler_key_rand_test,`sep_km_sram_scrambler_key_rand_test`>>
|3
|no_cpu, IMG-SCR, +skip_fuse_sense
|KM scrambler image with a preloaded parameter block (`HK-IMG-PARAM`) and mailbox report, scrambled SRAM write monitor and `KM-SRAM` observation tap
|Proves that the stored form of an SRAM word depends on key and address, that the old key reads its data back after a key change, that a key write after the lock is ignored, and that a lock set with the enable clear keeps the scrambler on.
|===

=== Test Procedures

Unless a procedure says otherwise, each test starts from the common
SEP bring-up for its run mode (`bring_up_no_cpu`,
`bring_up_and_wait_fuse_sense`, or `boot_firmware`), drives stimulus
through the agents above, and ends with zero UVM/scoreboard errors
plus the checkers listed here.

Each test entry carries these labelled fields, in this order: *Phase*,
*Area*, *Run mode*, *Source Module*, *BFM / Model*, *Randomization* (where
the entry states one), *Objective*, *Test Coverage*, *Test Procedure*,
*Checkers* and *Pass Criteria*. Optional notes (*Evidence*, *Anti-vacuity*,
*Scope*, *Not claimed* and others) follow *Pass Criteria*. *Area* is the
Test Summary table that lists the test. *Test Coverage* is one line: the cells
or path walked. *Pass Criteria* is that every named checker PASSes and
`UVM_ERROR` is 0. The checker table is the fail-able contract.

Some records come from shared bring-up code and the shared sequences, and an
entry does not repeat them. Each one fails the leaf that runs it:

* `CHK-FW-IDENTITY` (every firmware leaf): the loaded TCM images carry the
  `FW-BUILD-ID` of the firmware source at the run's commit.
* `CHK-FW-CONSOLE` (every firmware leaf): the firmware mailbox reports
  completion with the PASS magic.
* `CHK-ALERTS-ZERO` (leaves that bring up real entropy): CSRNG and EDN
  `ERR_CODE` and `RECOV_ALERT` read 0 at the end of the run.
* `CHK-NOISE-FORCE` (leaves that hold the ESRC noise constant):
  `+esrc_noise_force` routes `esrc_noise_ext_i` to lane 0, shown by driving
  both values.
* `CHK-DONE-RW1C` and `CHK-ERR` (every KMAC operation of the KMAC sequence):
  `INTR_STATE.kmac_done` sets after absorb and clears by W1C, and `ERR_CODE`
  and `INTR_STATE.kmac_err` read 0.

[[sep-vplan-phase3]]
=== Phase 3 Tests

Phase 3 tests prove the residual contracts of each area (<<sep-vplan-phase3-overview>>).
Each area has its Phase 3 summary rows in its table of the
Scenario-Expanded Test Summary, and its Phase 3 entries at the end of its
section of Test Procedures, after the entries of Phase 1 and Phase 2. The rows
are for human readers and the entries are for tools and audits. The two name
the same tests. An entry follows the field rules of Test Procedures and adds the fields
that its area section states. A Phase 3 test never overlaps a Phase 1 or
Phase 2 test. An entry that extends an open test names that test and the cell
that the entry adds. The Phase 3 contracts that no test can grade are in
<<sep-vplan-phase3-lists>>. The randomization class of every test is in
<<sep-vplan-phase3-random>>. Each Phase 3 leaf opens the coverage window of its
own entry only around its graded stimulus (<<sep-fcov-graded-window,SEP_FCOV>>).
The window gate is a testbench variable that only the coverage sampler reads; it
drives nothing and touches no DUT net. The sampler `u_sep_fcov` exists only on
VCS, so a Verilator leaf skips the gate write and runs the same checks.
Its bring-up, its control legs and a reset sequence between legs run with the
window closed, so they fill no Phase 3 bin, and neither does the traffic of
another test.

[[sep-vplan-phase3-overview]]
==== Phase 3 Overview

[cols="3,2,8",options="header"]
|===
|Area |Test numbers |Scope
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>
|1.25 to 1.53
|Crypto interrupt lines and cross-engine concurrency (two extension legs of open tests), HMAC, AES, KMAC, OTBN, ABR, delivered-key select, crypto alerts, ESRC health, FIFO integrity, autotune and conditioning, DRBG faults and sinks, TRNG stream handshake and external entropy to the Key Manager.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-fabric-remap,Fabric and remap>>
|2.25 to 2.33
|Inbound and outbound filter match with granule, default deny and source ID, global-to-local rebase, outbound route and output remap, alias remap, SMC route, extension port window, row responses with burst refusal, and DMA endpoint reach.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-mailbox-irq-pic,Mailbox, IRQ, PIC>>
|3.13 to 3.24
|Mailbox lines, FIFOs, errors, filter types and reset; PIC registers, priority, gateway cells, source map and external inputs; bridge fault latches and the fault-versus-clear race; timeout registers; NMI vector; timer and software interrupt tie-offs.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-spi-dma,SPI and DMA>>
|4.7 to 4.15
|SPI pad, FIFO, interrupt, error, strobe and reset contracts (one entry extends an open SPI test); DMA address modes, geometry, interrupt gate, abort, inline hash and SPI handshake. The DMA range-lock checks are blocked legs: they need a value that the specification does not state (<<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked-legs,Blocked legs>>).
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-mem-boot-reset,Memory, boot, reset, WDT, clock>>
|5.13 to 5.29
|Reset release, cold and CPU-only reset, software reset and isolation, JTAG and scan overrides, reset vector and run request, `SECURE_TM`, scratch and CPU control registers, watchdog, wakeup timer and reference counter, memory apertures and ECC, CPU demux, debug master, ISA and PMP.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-efuse-lifecycle,eFuse, lifecycle, SEC_DIS, locked-field>>
|6.18 to 6.30
|eFuse windows and fields, lifecycle code words, feature control, demotion and DFT gate ports, transient RMA, token channels, faults and latch freeze, interface status, lock slots, secure test mode, JTAG access, SEC_DIS timing, sense response and program interface faults.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-key-manager,Key Manager vault>>
|7.22 to 7.37
|Command framing, recovery and arguments; key slots, handles and allocator capacity; engine delivery and transfer policy; ABR shared key; boot and cold domain; SRAM load and handover; fault reports and SRAM latency; KMCSR, SRAM lock and reset domains; KPV; CPU trap and execute protection; crossbar and eFuse window; KM-side mailbox; DRBG sampler; OTP interface; engine key registers; SRAM scrambler.
|===

[[sep-vplan-phase3-parked]]
==== Phase 3 Parked Tests

A parked test has no graded check that runs without a PROPOSED item or an
unstated value (<<sep-vplan-credit-rules>>). Its entry carries the field
`*Needs*:`, its summary row reads "3, parked", it takes no credit, it is not in
an executable total, and its bins are blocked bins of `SEP_FCOV.adoc`
(<<sep-fcov-parked,Phase 2 Planned Groups>>).

No Phase 3 entry carries a `*Needs*:` field, so every Phase 3 test is
executable: at least one of its graded checks is not blocked. An entry names
the PROPOSED item or the unstated value of each of its blocked checks and legs.


[[sep-vplan-phase3-random]]
==== Phase 3 Randomization

Every Phase 3 stimulus that has a legal matrix is a pure function of the run
seed, and `--stage sim --seed N` replays the leaf. The stream is not a CSPRNG
and no drawn value is a key, a token or a nonce. No document quotes a per-seed
value as evidence. The class of a test is one of:

* `RAND-REP`: the seed draws points from a legal matrix, and every point has a stated outcome.
* `RANDCFG`: the seed draws a configuration, the test runs one scenario under it, and the expected outcome is a function of the configuration.
* `RAND-NONE`: the stimulus is directed, for the reason the entry states.

[[sep-vplan-phase3-drawn]]
*Drawn cells.* Regression seeds are random per leaf, and a leaf does not know
its run index, so no seed and no testlist entry pins a cell. A leaf draws its
cells from its seed (`SepSeededRng`, weighted toward the corners where the entry
says so, with the constraint filters of the entry). A leaf walks a cell set in
every run only when the set is cheap and contract-critical: a legal minimum or
maximum, one representative of each leg, or the error-injection case itself,
with no extra boot, no extra cold reset and no extra transfer of real size per
cell. The coverage bins of such cells fill in one `all --cov` regression. The
coverage bins of drawn cells are seed-dependent and close over a merge of
regressions, never in one regression (<<sep-fcov-rand-phase2,SEP_FCOV>>). For N
equally likely cells and d draws per leaf the expected closure is about
N * ln(N) / (d * reseed) regressions. The seed count of an entry stays small
(3 or 4) and is not raised to fill cells; an entry that runs more seeds states
the reason.

*Testlist entries.* A Phase 3 test has one testlist entry. One entry on
`lsu_stub_all_live` gives a Verilator leaf on that build and a VCS leaf on
`--target default`, so a test that grades some checks on one simulator only
splits them inside its one entry, and the leaf selects them from the simulator.
A second entry exists only when the run mode differs in a way that one entry
cannot carry: a time-0 bench plusarg that the prestage hook cannot set, or a
different run mode (`cpu` and `no_cpu`). No entry exists to pin a cell (no
per-cell variant, no cell or field plusarg). The tests with a second entry are
5.13 `sep_boot_release_terms_test`, whose entry
`sep_boot_release_terms_test_smc_hold` adds the bench plusarg
`+sep_smc_fuse_sense_hold`; 5.28 `sep_debug_master_target_matrix_test`, which
has one `cpu` entry and one `no_cpu` entry of the one module; and 7.28
`sep_km_fault_inject_rand_test`, which has one entry for each of its two
preloaded `rom_main` builds (HOST and HOST-UNREC, set by the t=0 plusarg
`+km_rom_hex`). An extension leg runs in the testlist entry of its
open test and adds no entry. Where the leg needs more seeds than that entry
runs, the entry takes the seed count of the leg (1.25 and 1.26).

[[sep-vplan-phase3-edn-waiver]]
*JTAG reset override waiver.* The JTAG `sep_reset_n` override and the per-IP
JTAG reset holds reset the crypto engines without the isolation sequence, so an
engine can drop an ungranted EDN request with no endpoint cancel, and the
`tb_top` crypto EDN request-hold assertions fire on a compliant DUT. Every
testlist entry whose leaf drives the `sep_reset_n` override (on the bench pins
`jtag_sep_reset_n_ovrd_i` / `jtag_sep_reset_n_val_i` or through the IC_RESET
TDR) or a per-IP hold `jtag_<ip>_rst_hold_i` adds `+sep_edn_jtag_reset_waive`.
The plusarg turns off only the crypto EDN arbiter and adapter request-hold
assertions (`tb/tb_top.sv`), and `tb_top` logs that it is set. The Phase 3
entries that add it are 5.17, 5.20, 5.22 and 6.29.

A test with two classes uses two of the three classes on different legs: a
drawn configuration for one leg and a matrix point for another, or a directed
leg next to a drawn leg. The entry names the dimensions of each class, and the
table below gives both classes of such a test. A row marked "extension" or
"extension leg" names an open test. Its class is the class of the cells that
the entry adds. A row marked "parked" names a parked test
(<<sep-vplan-phase3-parked>>).

[[sep-vplan-phase3-lists]]
==== Phase 3 Blocked Features, Observation Gaps and Scope

Each item is listed once. The area column names every area that the item
concerns. A blocked feature carries no Phase 3 credit, and random draws exclude
one. A feature with no observation point has no check that can fail on it. An
out of scope feature is graded by an open Phase 1 or Phase 2 test, or has no
stated pass or fail value, or belongs to another area. A PROPOSED internal
drive, an added hook and an observation tap that a test adds are listed in
<<sep-vplan-phase3-proposed>>. A check that needs a PROPOSED item is blocked
(<<sep-vplan-credit-rules>>). A check that needs only observation taps and added
hooks is not blocked.

[[sep-vplan-phase3-blocked]]
===== Blocked features

A blocked feature has no check in Phase 3, because the specification or the
register description does not state the value that a check needs. The
question names what the specification must state before a check can fail on
the feature. Where a test uses part of a blocked feature, the note says what
the test does.

[cols="3,5,6,4",options="header"]
|===
|Area |Feature |Open specification question |Note
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>

|DMA and CPU debug not-connected response
|What response does the DMA receive on its not-connected cells (DMA CSR, entropy pool, boot ROM), and what does the CPU debug master receive on its not-connected cells (boot ROM, CPU TCM)?
|`sep_fabric_dma_endpoint_matrix_test` (CHK-DMA-NOTCONN) grades that the request does not reach the target, only the rom_src and dma_csr_src legs, and only for runs that end in done or error. The rom_dst leg grades the PR-ROM silence and the dma_csr_src leg grades the PR-DMACSR silence, and PR-ROM and PR-DMACSR are observation taps the test adds to the SEP_TB_ARCH observation-probe list
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-fabric-remap,Fabric and remap>>
|Initiator arbitration and outstanding limits
|What is the arbitration policy between the five initiators, the outstanding limit per initiator and per target, and the starvation rule?
|-
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-fabric-remap,Fabric and remap>>
|Aperture register legal values
|What are the legal values of `SEP_GLOBAL_BASE_ADDR` and `SEP_REGION_SIZE` (alignment, power of two, size 0, upper bound)? The legal values of the SMU aperture registers are also unstated. `sep_fabric_inbound_rebase_test` uses fixed values only for the stated rule, and no leaf writes the SMU aperture registers.
|-
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-fabric-remap,Fabric and remap>>
|Effect time of a routing or filter CSR write
|Does a routing or filter CSR write take effect before the next transaction of the same initiator, and which setting applies to a transaction in flight? Every test puts a read-back between the write and the first dependent transaction.
|-
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-fabric-remap,Fabric and remap>>
|Source ID value of the CPU masters
|What is the numeric value of `SepSourceId`? The OTHERS value of zero is used only for remapped and DMA traffic.
|-
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-fabric-remap,Fabric and remap>>
|Overlapping alias remap entries
|Which entry applies when two or more valid alias remap entries cover one address? No draw overlaps valid entries.
|-
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-fabric-remap,Fabric and remap>>
|Beat shape of a blocked burst
|For a blocked burst, how many beats does the error subordinate return, where is RLAST, and does it echo the ID?
|-
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-fabric-remap,Fabric and remap>>
|Reset of the inbound filter `locked` bit and `entry_enabled`
|Does the SEP reset (`sep_reset_no`) return `FILTER_CONFIG.locked` and `entry_enabled` to their reset value 0? `reset_controller.adoc` states that `sep_reset_no` resets the system peripherals, `wdt_rst_ni` reaches only the CPU, `dbg_rstb_i` resets only the debug module and `scan_rst_ni` applies only while `test_en_i` is high. `fabric.adoc` places the filter entries in the system CSRs and does not say that the system CSRs are inside the system peripherals reset. Source: `hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc`; `hw/sys/sep/doc/fabric.adoc`; `hw/ip/axi_filter/regs/gen/adoc/filter_ctrl.adoc` (`FILTER_CONFIG`).
|`sep_mailbox_filter_type_matrix_test` (3.15) and `sep_mailbox_reset_clear_test` (3.17) lock an inbound filter entry. 3.17 grades only the ISS source ID restore by SEP reset (`mailbox.adoc`, Mailboxes) and logs the `locked` bit and `entry_enabled` after the reset
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-fabric-remap,Fabric and remap>>
|Ordering of one initiator's transactions
|Does the fabric keep two transactions of one initiator in order, for one target and for different targets? No leaf races two initiators on one address.
|-
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-fabric-remap,Fabric and remap>>
|CPU debug master gating
|Does `SEP_DBG` or any other control gate the CPU debug master access to the fabric?
|-
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-fabric-remap,Fabric and remap>>
|Writers of the lifecycle register block
|Which initiators may write the lifecycle controller register block, and are fabric reachability plus the inbound filter the only control?
|-
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-fabric-remap,Fabric and remap>>
|Source of inbound traffic
|Which masters drive `smn_inbound_axi_req_i`, and which `AxUSER` value does each master carry? The carrier of the source ID is not open: `hw/sys/smc/doc/assets/smc-traffic-filters.svg` (the decode of `axi_filter_wrap`) compares `src_id` with `AxUSER[3:0]`, and `hw/ip/axi_filter/doc/index.adoc` says the decode is identical in every instance. The fabric cells of `sep_fabric_filter_match_priority_rand_test` are graded on that figure.
|`sep_fabric_filter_match_priority_rand_test` drives the AxUSER value itself and grades no assumption about which master carries which value
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-fabric-remap,Fabric and remap>>

|Where does an address such as `0x0000_0000` to `0x0FFF_FFFF` go? Local requests in that class are logged and not graded. No address separates a moved SMU aperture from a fixed one, so no leaf writes `SMU_GLOBAL_BASE_ADDR` or `SMU_REGION_SIZE`.
|-
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-fabric-remap,Fabric and remap>>
|Burst to a register region outside the crypto region
|What does a burst to a register region outside the crypto region return? Bursts to register regions are not driven, so the burst-to-single conversion beyond two beats stays ungraded.
|-
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-fabric-remap,Fabric and remap>>
|CPU consequence of an error response
|What does the CPU core do when a load receives a DECERR or SLVERR response: does it raise a fault, and which one? No leaf compares a CPU fault state.
|-
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-fabric-remap,Fabric and remap>>
|Response ID at the boundary
|Does the response ID equal the request ID, and does the SI BID equal the AWID, across the ID-width changes of the fabric? No leaf grades the returned ID.
|-
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-fabric-remap,Fabric and remap>>
|SPI RX FIFO silence for refused accesses
|No stimulus fills the SPI RX FIFO under `no_cpu`, so a check that a refused SPI access never returns a FIFO word has nothing to leak. No leaf claims it.

|<<sep-vplan-phase3-mem-boot-reset,Memory, boot, reset>>
|DMA-path TCM ECC
|Does an ECC error on a DMA-initiated TCM read drive `ext_debug_bus_o[367:364]`, and what response code does a corrected single-bit DMA read return? (`hw/sys/sep/doc/cpu.adoc`.)

|<<sep-vplan-phase3-mem-boot-reset,Memory, boot, reset>>

|`sep_cpu_isa_pmp_csr_test` does not execute Zbe and does not drive the Zbf, Zbp or Zbr traps
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-mem-boot-reset,Memory, boot, reset>>
|Watchdog count unit
|Does `WDOG_COUNT` advance by one per `clk_wdt_i` period? The specification names `clk_wdt_i` as the timer clock and states no unit. Source: `hw/sys/sep/doc/watchdog.adoc`; `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/overlay/regs/aon_timer/regs/gen/adoc/aon_timer.adoc` (`WDOG_COUNT`).
|`sep_timer_clock_contract_test` grades count read-back relations only and logs the rate as `OBS-WDT-RATE`

|Does `sep_fuse_sense_done_o`, or `SEP_FUSE_SENSE_STATUS`, stay 1 after it rises while `rst_ni` is high? Source: `hw/sys/sep/doc/otp_fuse_controller.adoc` (Fuse Sensing and Boot Sequencing); `hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/adoc/blocks/sep_cpu_ctrl.adoc` (`SEP_FUSE_SENSE_STATUS`).
|No check grades stability; `sep_boot_release_terms_test` logs the poll as `OBS-SENSE-POLL`
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-mem-boot-reset,Memory, boot, reset>>

|How many counter increments and how many `ext_debug_bus_o[367:364]` pulses does one corrected TCM access give, given that the access is retried? Source: `hw/sys/sep/doc/interrupts.adoc` (memory ECC); `hw/sys/sep/doc/cpu.adoc`.

|ROM store response code
|What response code does a byte or halfword store to the ROM aperture return? `rom.adoc` says only that stores receive a response. Source: `hw/sys/sep/doc/rom.adoc`.
|`sep_boot_rom_aperture_sweep_test` grades that a response arrives and not its code
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-mem-boot-reset,Memory, boot, reset>>
|`SEP_CPU_CTRL.SMC_FUSE_SENSE_STATUS.smc_fuse_sense_done` is 1 only when SMC requests are safe, and the register description states that a request sent earlier hangs and gets no response.
|No specification question remains on the response: the register description states that a request to the SMC hangs while `smc_fuse_sense_done` is 0. How long a bench waits before it calls a request hung is an environment choice and not a specification value. Source: `hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/adoc/blocks/sep_cpu_ctrl.adoc` (`SMC_FUSE_SENSE_STATUS`); `hw/sys/sep/doc/port_table.adoc`.
|`sep_boot_release_terms_test` grades the register follow only and sends no outbound SMC request

|Per-field sense readiness

|<<sep-vplan-phase3-efuse-lifecycle,eFuse, lifecycle>>
|JTAG access policy outside PROD and RMA_SIP
|What read data does a blocked JTAG read return (the error response is stated, the data is not)? What is the JTAG policy in PROD_END, RMA_CHIPLET and INVALID, and before sense? Is `TOKEN_MATCH_FAULT` inside the token block? What may TRoot reach over JTAG? Does the JTAG policy of the lifecycle integrity error use the decoder that the bench injection reaches (`lc_sigint_inject_i` acts at the lifecycle decoder input)? (`sep_efuse_jtag_access_rand_test` draws only PROD and RMA_SIP, grades a non-OKAY response and does not read the shadow map over JTAG. The integrity-error leg of `sep_lcc_state_codeword_rand_test` (CHK-JTAG-SIGINT) uses the error inject `jtag_filter_lc_sigint_inject_i` at the decoder of the JTAG lifecycle filter, an added hook.)
|-
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-efuse-lifecycle,eFuse, lifecycle>>
|Concurrent AXI and JTAG requests
|What arbitration policy resolves concurrent AXI and JTAG requests, and what does the loser receive? (Every test serializes the two ports.)
|-
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-efuse-lifecycle,eFuse, lifecycle>>
|Software write class of fields with register access R
|Is a shadow field that the register description gives access R software-writable (field table) or read-only (register description)? `LC_STATE` has access R in the register description, and the field table gives it per-bit set-only rules with token gates. Phase 3 frontdoor writes exclude `LC_STATE`, so the per-bit rules of that write, its token gates, its terminal states, its lock exemption and its refusal under secure test mode or a comparator fault carry no grade. The same conflict holds for `BL1_VERSION` and `BL2_VERSION`: access R in `hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/adoc/blocks/sep_efuse_map.adoc` (address map rows `0x8C` and `0xAC`), set-only in the field table of `hw/sys/sep/doc/otp_fuse_controller.adoc`. Reads of these fields carry no grade: `sep_efuse_window_decode_reset_test` takes its control shadow write on `SYS_DIS`, and `sep_km_otp_interface_rand_test` writes `REQUIRED_ALGS` and `REQUIRED_SIGNERS`. Every shadow write of the Phase 3 tests goes to a field with access RW: `LOCKS`, `LOCKS_SPARE`, `SIP_DIS`, `SYS_DIS`, `REQUIRED_ALGS` and `REQUIRED_SIGNERS`.
|-
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-efuse-lifecycle,eFuse, lifecycle>>
|Result of a blocked read
|What does a read blocked by a read-lock return (zeros, bus data or error), and does it differ for AXI and JTAG? (`sep_efuse_lock_slot_matrix_rand_test` logs the response and the data of a blocked frontdoor read and grades the `efuse_req_error` flag of a read-interface request on the read-locked field.)
|-
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-efuse-lifecycle,eFuse, lifecycle>>
|Several go bits in one `TOKEN_EOP` write
|If one write sets several go bits, do the channels hash in parallel or by priority? (No test writes two go bits.)

|<<sep-vplan-phase3-efuse-lifecycle,eFuse, lifecycle>>
|Token-digest programming by lifecycle state
|Is token-digest programming restricted by lifecycle state, or is the write-lock the only rule?
|-
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-efuse-lifecycle,eFuse, lifecycle>>
|SEC_DIS revision-cell value
|What strap, port or parameter carries the SEC_DIS revision-cell value? (SEC_DIS acts only at the A0 value 1. Every SEC_DIS leaf assumes that value and proves it by the rise of `security_disable_o` at its first match.)

|<<sep-vplan-phase3-efuse-lifecycle,eFuse, lifecycle>>

(`sep_efuse_secure_tm_matrix_rand_test` grades the four fields of the SEP documents and no other field.)
|-
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-efuse-lifecycle,eFuse, lifecycle>>

|<<sep-vplan-phase3-efuse-lifecycle,eFuse, lifecycle>>
|Secure-boot enforcement in RMA_CHIPLET and INVALID

|<<sep-vplan-phase3-efuse-lifecycle,eFuse, lifecycle>>
|`REQUIRED_SIGNERS` write class
|Is the `REQUIRED_SIGNERS` shadow freely writable (field table) or set-only (register description, RW1S)?
|The Phase 3 shadow write of `REQUIRED_SIGNERS` writes the shadow value OR one new bit, which both readings give, so only the write-lock effect is graded. No order cell runs on slot 21.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-efuse-lifecycle,eFuse, lifecycle>>
|Development-key revoke check
|Must the ROM reject a PROD part whose development-key revoke bits are clear?

|Obfuscation of UIDs and `CLASS_KEY`

|<<sep-vplan-phase3-efuse-lifecycle,eFuse, lifecycle>>

Does a read-lock alone stop programming of the three UID fields (slots 11, 13, 15)? (`sep_efuse_lock_slot_matrix_rand_test` never draws the lock kind read alone on these slots.)

|Locked-field access interrupt

|Test enable and token freeze
|Which signal is test enable and what does the freeze do to a match in progress?
|Every SEC_DIS token go runs with `secure_tm_req_i` at 0; no test grades a match at strap 1 (`sep_lcc_feat_ctrl_state_matrix_rand_test`, `sep_efuse_secure_tm_matrix_rand_test`)
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-efuse-lifecycle,eFuse, lifecycle>>
|Demote and lock in one write
|If one write sets `demote` and `lock` together, does `demote` take effect? (`sep_lcc_feat_ctrl_state_matrix_rand_test` never writes `0x3`.)

|<<sep-vplan-phase3-efuse-lifecycle,eFuse, lifecycle>>
|`BL1_VERSION` and `BL2_VERSION` meaning
|Are `BL1_VERSION` and `BL2_VERSION` minimum-version values or independent flags? (`sep_efuse_lock_slot_matrix_rand_test` grades only the OR-merge and the shadow value, which both readings share. `sep_efuse_field_layout_rand_test` does not grade the two 256-bit fields.)
|-
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-efuse-lifecycle,eFuse, lifecycle>>
|Strap sample edge under SEC_DIS
|Which clock edge samples the strap when SEC_DIS holds at the early capture point? `test_mode.adoc` says the second rising clock edge after cold-reset release and `security_disable.adoc` says one cycle after release. The bench has no probe of the early capture, so the tests hold the strap low.
|-
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-efuse-lifecycle,eFuse, lifecycle>>
|`dbg_disable_t` layout
|What is the `dbg_disable_t` layout, and which of the 11 disables gates which DTP path? (`sep_lcc_feat_ctrl_state_matrix_rand_test` grades only the two fuse DFT ports.)
|-
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-efuse-lifecycle,eFuse, lifecycle>>
|Response for the token-window tail
|The response for an address inside the token window and past `0x1093_0573` is SLVERR in `hw/sys/sep/doc/otp_fuse_controller.adoc` and DECERR in `hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/adoc/memory_map.adoc`. (`sep_efuse_window_decode_reset_test` does not draw this span.)
|-
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-efuse-lifecycle,eFuse, lifecycle>>
|Digest word and byte order
|The specification does not state how the SHA-256 output is split into the shadow words and their bytes. (Phase 3 stages one convention: the FIPS 180-4 256-bit digest value, shadow word k holding bits [32k+31:32k]. It does not grade the packing and does not learn it from the DUT. The first Match step of a leaf is the control of the staging.)
|-
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-efuse-lifecycle,eFuse, lifecycle>>
|`LC_STATE` shadow reset value and pre-sense upper nibble
|The register description gives `LC_STATE` the reset value `0xF0`. The specification holds the INVALID state `4'b1111` before sense. No test grades the shadow reset value of `LC_STATE` or the upper nibble of `lc_state_o` before sense.
|-
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-efuse-lifecycle,eFuse, lifecycle>>
|`LC_STATE` write with a wrong upper nibble
|The specification does not state the effect of an `LC_STATE` shadow write with a wrong upper nibble. No test writes one.
|-
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-efuse-lifecycle,eFuse, lifecycle>>
|Write to demotion bits [63:2]
|The register description marks `DEMOTE_1` and `DEMOTE_2` bits [63:2] "Not used" and the specification states no effect for a write to them. No test writes them.
|-
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-efuse-lifecycle,eFuse, lifecycle>>
|Blocked CHIPLET match after a SiP match
|The specification does not say if a blocked CHIPLET match fires again after the SiP match sets bit 1. (`sep_efuse_transient_rma_rand_test` presents a CHIPLET mismatch go in between.)
|-
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-efuse-lifecycle,eFuse, lifecycle>>
|DFT fuse gate port polarity
|The polarity of `sep_fuse_dft_disable_o` and `smc_fuse_dft_disable_o` is not stated. (`sep_lcc_feat_ctrl_state_matrix_rand_test` names no level. It compares the two port values with each other by case class and grades the feature-control bit of each case against the inverse of its disable bit, which the specification states.)
|-
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-efuse-lifecycle,eFuse, lifecycle>>
|`lc_state_o` bit layout and polarity
|The specification states `lc_state_o` as differentially encoded and gives no bit layout and no polarity. The value `4'b1111` before sense is stated for the OTP shadow output into the life cycle controller, not for `lc_state_o`.
|No check reads `lc_state_o`
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-efuse-lifecycle,eFuse, lifecycle>>
|CPU-side response at the eFuse interface-control holes and the reserved span
|What exact response code and read data does the SEP CPU side see past the interface-control extent (0x1093_041C to 0x1093_04FF)? The response class "not OKAY" there is graded from `hw/sys/sep/doc/otp_fuse_controller.adoc` (section "OTP Fuse Controller", APB decode SLVERR). What response does the SEP CPU side see for the reserved span 0x1093_0600 to 0x1093_FFFF? No source states it: `hw/sys/sep/doc/memory_map.adoc` gives DECERR at the inbound port, and `hw/sys/sep/doc/otp_fuse_controller.adoc` sends that span to the shim bank control registers. The read data of the reserved span is not stated either, because the span goes to the shim bank control registers, whose contents the adopter defines. It is logged and not graded.
|`sep_unmapped_access_policy_test` grades the response past the interface-control extent. `sep_efuse_window_decode_reset_test` logs the response and the read data and grades no change of `STATUS`, `PROGRAM_CTRL`, `READ_CTRL` or a shadow word. The test does not claim that a past-extent or reserved access never answers OKAY
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-efuse-lifecycle,eFuse, lifecycle>>

|Does the eFuse guard drop a read or program command with an address of 8192 or more? The register description states only the status flags.
|`sep_efuse_interface_status_rand_test` grades the flags and logs the command count and image difference

|Program-request outcome at the program timeout

Source: `hw/ip/efuse/regs/gen/adoc/efuse_interface_ctrl.adoc` (`EFUSE_PROGRAM_REQ_TIMEOUT`, `EFUSE_PROGRAM_CTRL`).
|`sep_efuse_shim_response_fault_test` logs the timeout-enabled cell (`CHK-PROG-TIMEOUT INFO`) and does not grade it
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-efuse-lifecycle,eFuse, lifecycle>>
|Sense response status on lock, lifecycle, disable and last words

Source: `hw/sys/sep/doc/otp_fuse_controller.adoc` (Fuse Sensing and Boot Sequencing).
|`sep_efuse_shim_response_fault_test` draws words 10 to 254 only
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-key-manager,Key Manager>>
|ROM version value
|What ROM version does CMD_ROM_VER return? The specification states the layout {ROM_MAJOR[23:16], ROM_MINOR[15:8], ROM_PATCH[7:0]} and no value. (hw/ip/key_manager/doc/firmware.adoc, sections "0x00 - CMD_HW_VER", "0x01 - CMD_ROM_VER".)
|-
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-key-manager,Key Manager>>
|Handover inhibit after a rejected CMD_SRAM_LOAD_EXEC
|Does a rejected CMD_SRAM_LOAD_EXEC arm the handover inhibit? The text says the inhibit arms when CMD_EXEC_ROM succeeds and also that later handover commands fail when a handover command has already been issued. (hw/ip/key_manager/doc/firmware.adoc, sections "0x04 - CMD_RECOV_ACK", "0x10 - CMD_EXEC_ROM", "0x11 - CMD_SRAM_LOAD_EXEC".)
|-
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-key-manager,Key Manager>>
|Sideload shred pass count
|What is the numeric value of SHRED_ITER, so how many overwrite passes does a sideload shred run? (hw/ip/key_manager/doc/firmware.adoc, sections "Firmware Global Parameters", "Crypto Engine Drivers (HMAC / KMAC / AES / OTBN / Adams Bridge)".) `sep_km_key_transfer_rand_test` grades the order of the writes and the cleared key_valid only.
|-
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-key-manager,Key Manager>>
|Sampler beat with TSTRB 0x0
|What does the sampler do with a stream beat whose TSTRB is 0x0? (hw/ip/key_manager/doc/architecture.adoc, sections "DRBG Sampler", "Reads, prefetch and register access".) The check of the stated packing for non-zero TSTRB (CHK-SMP-PACK of `sep_km_drbg_sampler_rand_test`) needs the PROPOSED drive `HK-STRM-PAT`.
|-
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-key-manager,Key Manager>>
|Sampler read after a withdrawal in its first active cycle
|Does a withdrawal whose TREADY-low cycle is the first cycle of a pending DATA read, before the sampler enters the request state, end that read through the stream-error state? Does a withdrawal in the cycle in which the timeout expires also set TIMEOUT_ERR, and does it add one or two to COUNT_BAD? The text returns SLVERR on a stream error only if the active FSM is requesting or assembling, and gives the stream error priority over the timeout. (hw/ip/key_manager/doc/architecture.adoc, sections "Errors and reset", "DRBG Sampler", table "Key Manager DRBG sampler states".) `sep_km_drbg_sampler_rand_test` (CHK-SMP-STRM) holds TVALID low until the read ends, grades STREAM_ERR, DRBG_ERR, SLVERR with data 0 and COUNT_BAD + 1 or more, and logs TIMEOUT_ERR and the exact COUNT_BAD delta.
|-
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-key-manager,Key Manager>>
|IRQ_SET read response
|Does a read of IRQ_SET return 0 or SLVERR? The register summary says reads return 0 and the reserved-field row says reads trigger SLVERR. (hw/ip/key_manager/regs/gen/adoc/km_csr.adoc, sections "Key Manager Control and Status Registers", "SRAM_LOCK", "IRQ_SET"; hw/ip/key_manager/doc/architecture.adoc, section "Key Manager CPU Interrupt Processing".) `sep_km_kmcsr_reset_rand_test` writes IRQ_SET and never reads it.
|-
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-key-manager,Key Manager>>
|DEBUG and CTRL write response
|What response does a write of DEBUG or CTRL give? VERSION states SLVERR; DEBUG and CTRL state none. (hw/ip/key_manager/regs/gen/adoc/km_csr.adoc, sections "Key Manager Control and Status Registers", "VERSION", "CTRL", "DEBUG".) `sep_km_kmcsr_reset_rand_test` grades the stated read values only.
|-
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-key-manager,Key Manager>>
|VUART and TB_* registers in a production build
|Do VUART_TX, VUART_RX, VUART_STATUS and the eight TB_* registers exist in a production build, and what may they expose? (hw/ip/key_manager/doc/architecture.adoc, section "Reset Conditioning"; hw/ip/key_manager/regs/gen/adoc/km_csr.adoc, sections "Key Manager Control and Status Registers", "VUART_TX", "TB_CMD_RESULT".)
|-
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-key-manager,Key Manager>>
|Spurious interrupt code
|Which CPU IRQ bits are internal sources, so when is an IRQ bit unrecognised and `spurious_irq` (-12) raised? The specification names bits 3, 4 and 5 as external groups and does not number the others. (hw/ip/key_manager/doc/firmware.adoc, sections "Adams Bridge ML-KEM Shared Key", "Spurious IRQ"; hw/ip/key_manager/doc/architecture.adoc, section "Key Manager CPU Interrupt Processing".) No existing hook drives a Key Manager CPU interrupt line, so a check also needs a hook that drives one.
|no test drives code -12
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-key-manager,Key Manager>>
|Registered copy of the OTP readout
|Is there a registered copy of the OTP readout, and when does it reload? The architecture text says KM-local copies clear on reset and reload after release; the register text says every readout register is a read-through with no reset. (hw/ip/key_manager/doc/architecture.adoc, sections "Scan and Scan-Dump Protection", "OTP Inputs and Readout"; hw/ip/key_manager/regs/gen/adoc/km_csr.adoc, section "Key Manager Control and Status Registers".)
|-
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-key-manager,Key Manager>>
|Key derivation with the demotion state
|Does the KM derive keys, and which inputs include the demotion state? The lifecycle chapter says the KM includes DEMOTE_1 and DEMOTE_2 in key derivation; the KM chapter says ROM does not derive keys. (hw/sys/sep/doc/lifecycle_controller.adoc, section "Lifecycle States"; hw/ip/key_manager/doc/architecture.adoc, section "OTP Inputs and Readout".)
|-
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-key-manager,Key Manager>>
|Erase of a retired slot
|Does a repeated erase (CTRL write 0x4) of a retired slot rewrite its key words? The register text says an erase fills all 16 words and is never blocked; the architecture text says only that repeating the erase changes nothing. (hw/ip/key_manager/regs/gen/adoc/km_kpv.adoc, sections "CTRL[64]"; hw/ip/key_manager/doc/architecture.adoc, section "Key and Policy Vault (KPV)".) `sep_km_kpv_rand_test` grades the refusals after the repeated erase only.
|-
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-key-manager,Key Manager>>
|Fill value of a KPV erase
|Which value does a KPV erase write to the 16 words of a slot? The register text says that an LFSR fills all 16 words through the KPV scrambler, and the architecture text says pseudorandom data; neither states the LFSR polynomial, its seed, its state across erases or the word order, so no check can state the words after an erase or that they differ from the words before it. (hw/ip/key_manager/regs/gen/adoc/km_kpv.adoc, section "CTRL[64]", `erase`; hw/ip/key_manager/doc/architecture.adoc, section "Key and Policy Vault (KPV)".) `sep_km_kpv_rand_test` (CHK-KPV-ERASE, CHK-KPV-ERASE-RET), `sep_km_key_vault_alloc_rand_test` (CHK-REVOKE), `sep_km_boot_reset_rand_test` (CHK-SHRED-KPV) and `sep_km_handover_rand_test` (CHK-HO-KPV-SHRED, CHK-HO-KPV-LOCK) grade the stated CTRL and access effects of an erase and log the words.
|-
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-key-manager,Key Manager>>
|Physical KPV word position under the scrambler
|Which physical KPV storage index holds a logical key word while the KPV scrambler is enabled? The KPV text says that address scrambling maps the logical index to a scrambled physical index, and the scrambler text names the address permutation `perm10` and does not tabulate it; the ROM enables and locks the KPV scrambler at boot with a key from the DRBG. So no check can say which stored words belong to a slot. (hw/ip/key_manager/doc/architecture.adoc, section "KPV Scrambling"; hw/ip/scrambler/doc/architecture.adoc, section "Address Scrambling"; hw/ip/key_manager/doc/firmware.adoc, section "Boot Sequence".) `sep_km_key_vault_alloc_rand_test` (CHK-REVOKE) and `sep_km_handover_rand_test` (CHK-HO-KPV-SHRED) log the count of stored words that change and grade the CTRL effects only.
|-
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-key-manager,Key Manager>>
|Key-word positions of the DRBG pad
|Which key-word positions do the DRBG pad words of a short key fill, and in which order do the pad words and the mask words leave the DRBG stream? The text says only that short keys are padded with DRBG words and that an equally sized DRBG mask follows. (hw/ip/key_manager/doc/firmware.adoc, sections "Crypto Engine Drivers (HMAC / KMAC / AES / OTBN / Adams Bridge)", "DRBG Driver".) `sep_km_key_transfer_rand_test` grades the DRBG word count of a short transfer and logs the padded outputs.
|-
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-key-manager,Key Manager>>
|Property of a shuffled share order
|Which property of one shuffled share-write order, or of a set of such orders, is deterministic? The text states a Fisher-Yates shuffle that gives a uniformly random permutation from a PRNG whose state depends on every earlier PRNG call, so every order, the sequential order included, is legal. (hw/ip/key_manager/doc/firmware.adoc, sections "Firmware PRNG", "Register Access Shuffling".) `sep_km_key_transfer_rand_test` grades the address set and the last key_valid write and logs the orders.
|-
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-key-manager,Key Manager>>
|SEP flush during a blocked response send
|What happens to a response that the KM is writing when a SEP flush clears the software buffers? The text says a SEP flush resets the software buffers and counters and sends the fault frame, and that the response writer waits while the outbound buffer is full; it does not say whether the old response word is written after the flush. (hw/ip/key_manager/doc/firmware.adoc, sections "Error Recovery", "Outgoing Message Handler".) `sep_km_command_protocol_rand_test` flushes with the KM idle only.
|-
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-key-manager,Key Manager>>
|Secure test mode program request status
|Does the secure test block of a program request show as `program_done`, `program_status` or `efuse_req_error`? The text states that all fuse commands are blocked, that default all-zero responses are returned and that secure test mode contributes to the write lock status (`hw/ip/efuse/doc/architecture.adoc`, Secure Test Mode (`secure_tm_i`)). `efuse_interface_ctrl.adoc` defines `efuse_req_error` as a block by a read or program lock, and no sentence says whether secure test mode sets it. (`hw/ip/efuse/regs/gen/adoc/efuse_interface_ctrl.adoc`, `efuse_req_error`.)
|`sep_km_crossbar_efuse_window_rand_test` grades the zero read word, equal KM and host results and an unchanged fuse bit, and logs the tuple
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-key-manager,Key Manager>>
|SRAM scrambler lock with the enable clear
|What does `SCRAMBLER_CTRL.enable` read after the lock is set while enable is 0? The register text says key and enable cannot be modified after the lock and does not say which value the enable bit reads; the scrambler text says the scrambler is forced into the enabled state. (`hw/ip/key_manager/regs/gen/adoc/km_csr.adoc`, `SCRAMBLER_CTRL`; `hw/ip/scrambler/doc/architecture.adoc`, Integration in the Key Manager.)
|`sep_km_sram_scrambler_key_rand_test` grades the forced scrambling by the scrambled-write count and logs the read value
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-key-manager,Key Manager>>
|Extent of each SRAM shred pass of the fatal handler
|`hw/ip/key_manager/doc/firmware.adoc`, Error Recovery, states one Weyl pass and two xoshiro128++ passes of the SRAM and does not state the extent of each pass. The full 0x8000 to 0xFFFF extent is stated only for the handover scramble.
|`sep_km_fault_inject_rand_test` grades at least 8192 SRAM writes and logs the count and the 24576 value of three full passes
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-key-manager,Key Manager>>
|`invalid_len` (-5) argument when `PAYLOAD_LEN` contradicts the frame length
|How is the received payload length of the `invalid_len` (-5) argument counted when `PAYLOAD_LEN` contradicts the frame length on the wire? (hw/ip/key_manager/doc/firmware.adoc, section "0x00 - RESP_CMD".)
|`sep_km_command_protocol_rand_test` grades the -5 code and logs the argument
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-key-manager,Key Manager>>
|`OTP_DEMOTION_STATE` group encoding
|What are the bit order and polarity of the two `OTP_DEMOTION_STATE` groups {demote_2[1:0], demote_1[1:0]}, that is, which code means "not demoted"? (hw/ip/key_manager/doc/architecture.adoc, section "OTP Inputs and Readout".)
|`sep_km_otp_interface_rand_test` grades complementary groups and a change from the recorded value
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-key-manager,Key Manager>>
|Level bits of the Key Manager mailbox interrupt status after reset
|Which value does a read after reset return for `KM_IRQ_STATUS.outbound_write_space_avail`, `KM_IRQ_STATUS.inbound_read_data_avail` and the level bits of `SEP_IRQ_STATUS`? The register description gives the reset value 0x0, and the field text says level 1 means space available, with both FIFOs empty at reset. (hw/ip/key_manager/regs/gen/adoc/km_mailbox_km.adoc, `KM_IRQ_STATUS`; hw/ip/key_manager/regs/gen/adoc/km_mailbox_sep.adoc, `SEP_IRQ_STATUS`.)
|`sep_km_mailbox_km_side_rand_test` logs the level bits at reset and grades them after the first FIFO event
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-key-manager,Key Manager>>
|A shredded or revoked engine holds no key (`key_valid` 0). The output of an AES in that state is not stated.
|What does an engine output after its `key_valid` is cleared (no output, an error, or an output under a zero key)? Source: `hw/sys/sep/doc/aes.adoc` (`CTRL_SHADOWED.sideload` states the selection only); `hw/ip/key_manager/doc/firmware.adoc` ("Key Transfer and Sideload Purge").
|`sep_km_key_transfer_rand_test`, `sep_km_boot_reset_rand_test` and `sep_km_fault_inject_rand_test` grade the absence of a key on the HMAC witness only: its no-key result is the digest of its software key, which the HMAC text states. An AES output that differs from the golden of the delivered key, or no output, is logged as `OBS-AES-NOKEY` and is never counted as a pass of a refusal, a shred or a revoke; an AES output that equals the golden where no key is expected fails. They do not claim that the AES gives no output.
|===

[[sep-vplan-phase3-blocked-legs]]
====== Blocked legs of tested features

A tested feature can have one blocked leg. The test grades the rest of the
feature, and the note says how the test treats the blocked leg.

[cols="3,5,6,4",options="header"]
|===
|Area |Item |Blocked leg and open question |Note
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>
|RECOV_ALERT_STS has nine bits cleared by writing zero; the three CMD_STAGE alerts ignore the bad command.
|RECOV_ALERT_STS bits other than CMD_STAGE_INVALID_CMD_SEQ_ALERT: bit 13 needs an illegal ACMD, bit 15 needs the reseed enforcement, bits 0 to 4 need a stimulus that writes an invalid multi-bit boolean (True = 6 and False = 9 are stated, and no Phase 3 entry drives an invalid value), bit 12 needs an attack pattern. Source: `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/overlay/regs/csrng/regs/gen/adoc/csrng.adoc` (`RECOV_ALERT_STS`).
|`sep_drbg_cmd_sequence_rand_test` grades bit 14
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>
|BOOT_INS_CMD (reset 0x901) and BOOT_GEN_CMD (reset 0xFFF003) must use clen 0 or EDN hangs.
|BOOT_INS_CMD or BOOT_GEN_CMD with clen other than 0 hangs EDN. The position of clen is not stated. Source: `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/overlay/regs/edn/regs/gen/adoc/edn.adoc` (`CTRL`, `BOOT_INS_CMD`, `BOOT_GEN_CMD`).
|the reset values are used; no clen injection
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>
|AES `TRIGGER.DATA_OUT_CLEAR` and `TRIGGER.KEY_IV_DATA_IN_CLEAR` clear the named registers with pseudo-random data, and the engine takes a new key, IV and plaintext after the clear.
|The value after a clear: the specification states pseudo-random data, no value of DATA_OUT or IV after the clear, and no status bit that the clear sets or clears. AES: state a deterministic effect of each clear trigger that software can read. Source: `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/overlay/regs/aes/regs/gen/adoc/aes.adoc` (`TRIGGER`, `STATUS`); `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/upstream/hw/ip/aes/data/aes.hjson` (`TRIGGER`, `IV`, `DATA_OUT`).
|`sep_aes_reseed_trigger_rand_test` logs the values after both clears (CHK-AES-CLEAR-OUT is blocked) and grades the encryption after the key clear and a reload (CHK-AES-CLEAR-KEY)
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>
|PERIPH_BUS_ERR_STATUS has one bit each for aes, hmac, kmac, otbn, csrng and edn (plus wdt). The bit is the wrapper's sticky bridge error, latches on a TL-UL error response, is cleared by the matching PERIPH_BUS_ERR_CLEAR bit and ORs into PIC source 43.
|AES, CSRNG, EDN and WDT bits of PERIPH_BUS_ERR_STATUS. The spec says the bits latch on a TL-UL error and states no access that makes these wrappers return one. Source: `hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/adoc/blocks/sep_cpu_ctrl.adoc` (`DMA_BUS_ERR_CLEAR`, `PERIPH_BUS_ERR_STATUS`, `PERIPH_BUS_ERR_CLEAR`); `hw/sys/sep/doc/crypto.adoc` (OpenTitan Alert Termination).
|`sep_cpu_bridge_fault_matrix_rand_test` drives HMAC, KMAC and OTBN
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>
|`ERR_BITS` is cleared by a write of any value while OTBN is not running, and a write while OTBN is running is ignored (`otbn.hjson`, `ERR_BITS`). Bits [7:0] hold eight software-class bits, and bit 8 `mai_software_error` is a ninth.
|A way to set a non-zero `ERR_BITS`: the clear rule is stated, and no sentence states how software sets a bit, except the `KEY_INVALID` error of an invalid delivered key (`hw/sys/sep/doc/crypto.adoc`, delivered-key table). Without a stated way to set it, a check of the clear cannot fail. Source: `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/upstream/hw/ip/otbn/data/otbn.hjson` (`ERR_BITS`); `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/overlay/regs/otbn/regs/gen/adoc/otbn.adoc` (`STATUS`, `ERR_BITS`).
|No entry grades the clear. `sep_otbn_cmd_status_rand_test` reads `ERR_BITS` only to expect 0 and never clears it
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>
|The noise and BIW observe FIFOs discard a word while full and set an overflow bit.
|ESRC: when an observe FIFO is full, is the incoming word or the oldest word the one that is discarded? Source: `hw/ip/entropy_source/regs/gen/adoc/entropy_source.adoc` (`INTR_STATUS`, `NOISE_OBS_CTRL`, `BIW_OBS_CTRL`).
|`sep_esrc_observe_fifo_rand_test` grades the plateau level, the overflow bit and its write-one clear only
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>
|The stream port of the main FIFO carries each word as it is written into the FIFO with no back-pressure, and a write while full is discarded.
|ESRC: does a word whose write is discarded while the FIFO is full appear on the stream? Source: `hw/ip/entropy_source/doc/architecture.adoc` (Output FIFO); `hw/ip/entropy_source/regs/gen/adoc/entropy_source.adoc` (`FIFO_STATUS`, `INTR_STATUS`).
|`sep_esrc_fifo_flow_rand_test` logs the stream word count while the FIFO is full and grades nothing on it
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>
|ESRC `INTR_STATUS` reports the latched interrupt status of eight sources, and `INTR_ENABLE` enables each source.
|ESRC: does an `INTR_STATUS` bit latch while its `INTR_ENABLE` bit is clear, or does the enable gate the source into the status? Source: `hw/ip/entropy_source/regs/gen/adoc/entropy_source.adoc` (`INTR_STATUS`, `INTR_ENABLE`, `INTR_TEST`).
|Every ESRC entry that grades a status bit writes its enable bit 1 after each TRNG reset (crypto common rule "ESRC interrupt status"). `sep_esrc_csr_rw_walk_rand_test` logs the status under a clear enable bit and grades nothing on it. FCOV `x_esrc_status_enable` has no bin for the (status 1, enable 0) cells
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>
|KMAC CFG_SHADOWED.mode selects cSHAKE, and the encoded name and customization prefix is written by software into the PREFIX registers.
|KMAC: which encoded name and customization prefix gives the digest golden of an unkeyed cSHAKE cell, and how does the specification define the encoding? Source: `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/overlay/regs/kmac/regs/gen/adoc/kmac.adoc` (`PREFIX_0`, `CFG_SHADOWED`).
|`sep_kmac_stream_cfg_rand_test` drives SHA3 and SHAKE only
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>
|A second write with a different value to a shadowed register sets the update error. `aes.hjson` states for AES `CTRL_SHADOWED` and `CTRL_AUX_SHADOWED` that a read clears the phase tracking, so the next write is a first write.
|KMAC: does a read of a shadowed register between the two writes restart the write sequence? `kmac.hjson` does not say. Source: `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/overlay/regs/kmac/regs/gen/adoc/kmac.adoc` (`STATUS`); `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/upstream/hw/ip/kmac/data/kmac.hjson`.
|`sep_crypto_shadow_update_err_rand_test` issues no read between the two writes
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>
|The ABR sequencer parks with STATUS.VALID set after a result, and STATUS.READY re-asserts after a zeroize or a reset.
|ABR: does STATUS.VALID clear when READY re-asserts after a zeroize or an ABR reset? Source: `hw/sys/sep/doc/adams_bridge.adoc`; `integration/rdl/sep/abr_reg.rdl` (`STATUS`).
|`sep_abr_command_matrix_rand_test` grades READY and logs VALID
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>
|Invalid encodings map to safe values: MODE to AES_NONE, KEY_LEN to AES_256, OPERATION to AES_ENC, PRNG_RESEED_RATE to PER_1, PHASE to GCM_INIT, NUM_VALID_BYTES to 16.
|Readback of an invalid encoding. The RDL does not say whether the readback shows the written or the mapped value. Source: `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/overlay/regs/aes/regs/gen/adoc/aes.adoc` (`CTRL_SHADOWED`).
|`sep_aes_encoding_inject_rand_test` grades behaviour and logs the readback
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-spi-dma,SPI and DMA>>
|STATUS reset value
|The RDL reset column states 0x0 for READY, TXEMPTY and RXEMPTY (`spi_controller.adoc`, STATUS). The Phase 1 and Phase 2 reset check requires them set. Which one is the contract?
|Effect: The tests read STATUS at run time and never compare it with a literal
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-spi-dma,SPI and DMA>>
|SPI RX watermark meaning
|Does RXWM mean "reaches" or "exceeds" the threshold (`spi_controller.adoc`, CONTROL, STATUS, EVENT_ENABLE)?
|Effect: Equality is never driven (`sep_spi_fifo_stall_watermark_rand_test`, `sep_spi_ot_host_csr_irq_rand_test`). `sep_spi_dma_handshake_rand_test` keeps C/4 at or below RX_WATERMARK
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-spi-dma,SPI and DMA>>
|DMA RANGE_REGWEN false value
|Which write gives the RANGE_REGWEN false value, given RW0C (`secure_dma.adoc`, RANGE_REGWEN)? A write-zero-to-clear from the reset value 0x6 cannot give the False encoding 0x9.
|Effect: CHK-DMA-LOCK of `sep_dma_irq_error_lock_rand_test` is blocked. The leaf writes 0x9, logs the read-backs as `OBS-DMA-LOCK` and grades nothing. `sep_cpu_reset_retention_test` writes 0, grades only the stored value 0x0 across a CPU-only reset and its return to 0x6 at a cold reset, and claims no lock effect
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-spi-dma,SPI and DMA>>
|Partial strobe merge
|What is the merge rule for a partial strobe inside a half (`spi.adoc`, Features)?
|Effect: Partial strobes are not driven (`sep_spi_ot_host_csr_irq_rand_test`)
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-spi-dma,SPI and DMA>>
|Short or oversized final chunk
|What does a short final chunk or a chunk larger than the total do (`secure_dma.adoc`, CHUNK_DATA_SIZE)?
|Effect: Not driven
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-spi-dma,SPI and DMA>>
|INCREMENT=0 with WRAP=0 and two or more chunks
|Where does a later chunk start when INCREMENT=0 and WRAP=0, given "not changed" for INCREMENT=0 and "contiguous ascending addresses" for WRAP=0 (`secure_dma.adoc`, SRC_CONFIG, DST_CONFIG)?
|Effect: Driven with one chunk only (`sep_dma_transfer_matrix_rand_test`)
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-spi-dma,SPI and DMA>>
|Masked SPI error
|Does a masked SPI error still set its status bit (`spi_controller.adoc`, ERROR_ENABLE, ERROR_STATUS)?
|Effect: Log-only (`OBS-SPI-MASK`)
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-spi-dma,SPI and DMA>>
|Forced SPI_EVENT release
|How does a forced SPI_EVENT release (`spi_controller.adoc`, INTR_TEST)?
|Effect: Not graded
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-spi-dma,SPI and DMA>>
|DMA interrupt clear write
|When does the clear write of CLEAR_INTR_SRC land, and which bus encoding selects which port (`secure_dma.adoc`, CLEAR_INTR_SRC, INTR_SRC_ADDR_0)?
|Effect: Not driven (`sep_spi_dma_handshake_rand_test`). CLEAR_INTR_BUS resets to 0, which selects a tied-off address space that does not complete (`dma.adoc`, OCAH Modifications)

[[sep-vplan-phase3-unstated]]
====== Unstated cells that block no feature

<<sep-vplan-phase3-mailbox-irq-pic,Mailbox, IRQ, PIC>>:

* The response of a write of 0 to a W 1P clear register is not stated beyond "write 1 to clear". `sep_cpu_bridge_fault_matrix_rand_test` grades that 0 leaves the latch set and does not grade the response code.

<<sep-vplan-phase3-fabric-remap,Fabric and remap>>:

* The response of a Reserved, Past Extent or Hole in Extent cell as seen by the CPU LSU or the DMA. `hw/sys/sep/doc/memory_map.adoc` states the cells for a 32-bit access as seen at the inbound AXI port, and says what the SEP CPU sees only for the boot ROM, reset control, ICCM, DCCM, PIC and AP/STEE rows. The fabric leaves grade the reset-control gaps, tails and past-extent cells from the LSU against the table, because the specification states the CPU view for those rows. For every other row, the fabric leaves grade the cells through the System Interface and log the LSU value. The response of the fabric to a DMA-master access at a reserved window is not stated either, so no address can stand in for the non-OKAY response control of the outbound responder. The non-OKAY leg of the DMA host-path latch in `sep_cpu_bridge_fault_matrix_rand_test` uses that control, an error inject that the test adds (see <<sep-vplan-phase3-added-hooks,Hooks that the tests add>>).
* The response and read data of a 1-, 2- or 8-byte access to a Reserved, Past Extent or Hole in Extent cell. The table states the cells only for a 32-bit access. The row leaf grades AxSIZE 2 in both halves of the bus and logs AxSIZE 3.

<<sep-vplan-phase3-mem-boot-reset,Memory, boot, reset>>:

* The assertion timing of `sep_intermediate_reset_n`, and so of `sep_reset_n`, after the `rst_ni` fall. `hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc` (SEP Reset Controller) states that the eFuse wrapper synchronizes the AND of the boot terms into `sep_intermediate_reset_n` and that `u_sep_reset_sync` asserts its input asynchronously, and gives no assertion behaviour for the first synchronizer. `sep_cold_reset_phase_test` logs the delay as `OBS-RST-ASSERT` and grades the release only.

<<sep-vplan-phase3-key-manager,Key Manager vault>>:

* The reserved bits of CMD_KEY_REVOKE, CMD_KEY_TRANSFER and CMD_ENGINE_SHRED, the transfer mask 0 and the shred mask 0. (hw/ip/key_manager/doc/firmware.adoc, sections "0x22 - CMD_KEY_GENERATE", "0x25 - CMD_ENGINE_SHRED".) The tests keep the reserved bits clear and never draw them.
* The return code of CMD_KEY_REVOKE for an unavailable handle, and the return code of any command for a null or never-issued handle. (hw/ip/key_manager/doc/firmware.adoc, section "0x23 - CMD_KEY_REVOKE".) A CMD_KEY_TRANSFER of a revoked handle is a failure (section "Key Transfer and Sideload Purge") and `sep_km_key_transfer_rand_test` grades it.
* The return argument of CMD_KEY_GENERATE when both REQ_SIZE and DEST_VALID are invalid. (hw/ip/key_manager/doc/firmware.adoc, section "0x22 - CMD_KEY_GENERATE".) `sep_km_command_protocol_rand_test` logs it and does not grade it.
* The read response of a write-only register (KM_WRITE_DATA and the key-share registers). `sep_km_crossbar_efuse_window_rand_test` logs it and does not grade it.
* The TIMEOUT_ERR and COUNT_BAD results when a stream error and a timeout expiry fall in one cycle (STREAM_ERR set is stated). (hw/ip/key_manager/doc/architecture.adoc, section "Errors and reset".) `sep_km_drbg_sampler_rand_test` logs them and does not grade them.
* The precedence of the handover-disabled and zero-size conditions of CMD_SRAM_EXEC. (hw/ip/key_manager/doc/firmware.adoc, section "0x12 - CMD_SRAM_EXEC".)
* The CMD_SRAM_LOAD_EXEC image ceiling value. `sep_km_handover_rand_test` grades sizes at or above the SRAM size and logs the rest.
* The read value of a locked KPV_SCRAMBLER_KEY. (hw/ip/key_manager/regs/gen/adoc/km_kpv.adoc, sections "CTRL[64]", "KPV_SCRAMBLER_KEY".)
* The value of OTP_EFUSE_REMAP_BASE. (hw/ip/key_manager/doc/architecture.adoc.) `sep_km_crossbar_efuse_window_rand_test` grades that the base is constant.
* The OTP readout value before the sense completes, and the length of the OTP change suppression window. (hw/sys/sep/doc/otp_fuse_controller.adoc, section "Fuse Sensing and Boot Sequencing"; hw/ip/key_manager/doc/architecture.adoc, section "OTP Inputs and Readout".)
* Whether SEP_IRQ_STATUS.flushed_by_km sets at the boot flush of the ROM. (hw/ip/key_manager/regs/gen/adoc/km_mailbox_sep.adoc, section "SEP_IRQ_STATUS".) `sep_km_command_protocol_rand_test` logs it.
* The byte-wise permutation (perm8) of the KM SRAM scrambler and the address permutations (perm10, perm13) are not tabulated in hw/ip/scrambler/doc/architecture.adoc. No cipher golden exists for the KM SRAM stored word. `sep_km_sram_scrambler_key_rand_test` grades the KM SRAM by key, address and bijection, and `sep_km_kpv_rand_test` grades the cipher on the KPV path (whole-word permutation) and logs the KPV cells read after a cold reset, because the stored words then lie at the indices that perm10 selects.

[[sep-vplan-phase3-noobs]]
===== Features with no observation point

A disposition says what is done about the missing observation. `ADD-PORT`: a
testbench port, drive or hook that the bench lacks would give the observation,
and the DUT does not change. `ACCEPT-PROBE`: a read-only probe or hierarchical
reference, or a firmware CSR read, is the observation. A probe or reference that
`hw/sys/sep/dv/docs/SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc` does not list is an observation tap the
test adds to the SEP_TB_ARCH observation-probe list, and a check on it is
executable. `PERMANENT-HOLE`: no observation exists at SEP level, so no check can
fail on the contract.

A read-only probe, tap or reference is an observation tap and is not PROPOSED.
A port, drive, inject or hook that exists in `hw/sys/sep/dv/tb/tb_top.sv` or that
`SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc` lists is not PROPOSED, and the row says so. An error inject
and a drive of a port of the `sep` module are added hooks and are not PROPOSED
(<<sep-vplan-credit-rules>>). A drive, force or deposit of a net inside `sep`
for a purpose other than error injection is PROPOSED.
<<sep-vplan-phase3-proposed>> lists each observation tap, each added hook and
each PROPOSED item with the tests that use it. A check that needs a PROPOSED
item is blocked (<<sep-vplan-credit-rules>>).

[cols="3,4,4,2,8",options="header"]
|===
|Area |Feature |Missing observation |Disposition |Reason
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>
|Secret keys travel only on the private Key Manager AXI4-Lite key buses. Host data and CSR traffic uses the separate AXI4 bus from the SEP fabric.
|-
|PERMANENT-HOLE
|The key buses have no SEP port and no register view. The delivered-key outcome is graded end to end by the open Key Manager tests.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>
|Alert channels: HMAC [0], OTBN [2:1], AES [4:3], KMAC [6:5], CSRNG [8:7], EDN [10:9]. ABR and ESRC have no alert channel.
|-
|ACCEPT-PROBE
|Per-index alert wires (the 11 receiver pulses). The alert receiver probe (`crypto_alert_pulse`, read-only, 11 bits; named `crypto_alert_pulse_probe_o` at the boundary) is an observation tap the test adds to the SEP_TB_ARCH observation-probe list. It serves `sep_pic_pending_map_walk_rand_test` (CHK-ALERT-IDX). The aggregate leg is a register and vector check.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>
|The crypto complex marks 11 Class 2 scan roots: ten inside sep_crypto (hmac, otbn, aes, kmac, abr wrappers, entropy source, drbg, crypto EDN adapter, pool EDN adapter, key manager) plus u_trng_wrapper_s3c_scan. The integration module, public bus infrastructure and combinational entropy muxes are not roots.
|-
|PERMANENT-HOLE
|Structural scan-boundary list. No SEP-level observation.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>
|The OTBN OTP scramble key handshake is tied to fixed values.
|-
|PERMANENT-HOLE
|Tie-off of the OTBN OTP key handshake. No SEP-level net.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>
|OTBN life-cycle escalation and RMA requests are tied inactive, so lifecycle_escalation never sets from the life-cycle controller.
|-
|ACCEPT-PROBE
|Probe the OTBN life-cycle escalation and RMA input pins and expect a constant 0. `sep_otbn_cmd_status_rand_test` reads ERR_BITS and FATAL_ALERT_CAUSE as a passive floor. No control window exists, because software cannot raise an escalation.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>
|The OTBN core is the full implementation (Stub = 0) with a flop register file (RegFileFF).
|-
|PERMANENT-HOLE
|Build parameters (Stub = 0, RegFileFF). No SEP-level observation.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>
|AES life-cycle escalation is tied inactive, so that fatal-fault cause cannot occur.
|-
|ACCEPT-PROBE
|Probe the AES life-cycle escalation input pin and expect a constant 0. The STATUS.ALERT_FATAL_FAULT floor has no control window.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>
|The adapter forwards the per-beat FIPS bit to AES edn_fips. AES does not use it, so both values behave the same.
|-
|PERMANENT-HOLE
|AES ignores the FIPS tag. The tag is 1 on every path (seed adapter and external mux), so a value of 0 can never be driven and the two behaviours cannot be compared.

|A stimulus for a KM-side mailbox overflow or underflow in the production ROM (recoverable codes -3 and -4, and the outbound-overflow and inbound-underflow legs of the mailbox ISR order)

|PERMANENT-HOLE
|The state is seeded from DRBG words and written into the scrambled SRAM. No output relation can be checked without the scrambler key and the seed.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-key-manager,Key Manager>>
|Adopter DFT test-command class
|The admitted test-command class of the adopter DFT controller
|PERMANENT-HOLE
|The adopter integration owns command admission (hw/ip/key_manager/doc/architecture.adoc, sections "Wipe State", "Scan and Scan-Dump Protection"). The `secure_tm_o` port is sampled in `sep_km_otp_interface_rand_test`.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-key-manager,Key Manager>>
|CPU interrupt mask restore and RX and TX indices
|The CPU interrupt mask restore and the RX and TX buffer indices
|PERMANENT-HOLE
|Internal CPU and firmware RAM state. The order and no-loss behavior are graded in `sep_km_command_protocol_rand_test`.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-key-manager,Key Manager>>
|DMA access initiator at the crypto target
|The initiator tag and address of a DMA access at the crypto target
|ACCEPT-PROBE
|The check needs an initiator tag at the crypto target and a firmware-driven secure DMA read of the KM aperture in run mode `cpu`. The DMA tests own this leg; the Key Manager scope records no credit for it.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-key-manager,Key Manager>>
|Isolation of the AES, OTBN and eFuse paths
|The isolation request and acknowledge of the AES, OTBN and eFuse paths, the drain of accepted transactions and the order against the warm reset
|ADD-PORT
|Only three isolation probes exist (HMAC, KMAC, ABR). `sep_km_kmcsr_reset_rand_test` grades those three. The reset controller tests own the sequencer.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-key-manager,Key Manager>>
|KM SRAM scratch locations of key material
|KM SRAM scratch locations of key material
|PERMANENT-HOLE
|The scratch lives in the scrambled SRAM at addresses the specification does not state. A test cannot search the array for plaintext.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-key-manager,Key Manager>>
|The allocator never uses a read-locked, sealed or retired slot.
|A stimulus that sets seal or lock_use on a slot before an allocation
|PERMANENT-HOLE
|No ROM flow and no host command sets seal or lock_use before an allocation. The allocator capacity oracle (CHK-CAP of `sep_km_key_vault_alloc_rand_test`) covers the live-key and revoked-slot cases only.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-key-manager,Key Manager>>
|A slot-write error during CMD_KEY_LOAD destroys the handle registration, keeps the earlier vault writes and does not reuse the handle.
|A failure of one KPV slot write
|ADD-PORT
|The slot-write failure needs an injected KPV write failure. No inject of `tb_top.sv` and no added hook of a Phase 3 entry provides it, and no entry grades the contract.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-key-manager,Key Manager>>
|CMD_KEY_TRANSFER stops at the first driver failure and keeps the earlier engine writes.
|A failure of one engine key write
|ADD-PORT
|The failure needs an injected engine-write failure. No inject of `tb_top.sv` and no added hook of a Phase 3 entry provides it, and no entry grades the contract.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-key-manager,Key Manager>>
|The OTP change interrupt flow of the ROM.
|The source is not enabled at boot, so the flow is dormant in the ROM.
|PERMANENT-HOLE
|No stimulus reaches a dormant flow without a firmware change.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-key-manager,Key Manager>>
|The allocator starts at a DRBG-selected slot.
|The start slot of an allocation
|PERMANENT-HOLE
|No net shows the DRBG-selected start slot. The capacity oracle does not depend on it.
|===

[[sep-vplan-phase3-proposed]]
===== Observation taps, added hooks and proposed drives

This section has three lists. <<sep-vplan-phase3-obs-taps>> lists the read-only
observations that Phase 3 tests use and that `hw/sys/sep/dv/docs/SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc`
does not list: each is an observation tap the test adds to the SEP_TB_ARCH
observation-probe list. <<sep-vplan-phase3-added-hooks>> lists the error injects
and the top-port drives that Phase 3 tests use and that `SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc` does
not list: each is an added hook that the test adds to the inject exceptions or
to the HDL Top port table of `SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc`. It also lists the existing
bench hooks of `hw/sys/sep/dv/tb/tb_top.sv` that Phase 3 tests use and that
`SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc` does not list. An observation tap, an added hook and an
existing bench hook are not PROPOSED, so a check or bin that needs only these is
executable (<<sep-vplan-credit-rules>>). <<sep-vplan-phase3-proposed-drives>> lists the
PROPOSED items: each drives, forces or deposits into a net inside the `sep`
module for a purpose other than error injection, and it is not in
`SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc` and not in `hw/sys/sep/dv/tb/tb_top.sv`. No item changes DUT
RTL. A check that needs a PROPOSED item is blocked: it is not a pass and takes no
credit. An item with two uses is one row in each list that applies. A preload
(<<sep-vplan-credit-rules>>) is part of the approved t=0 image load and is in
none of the three lists; <<sep-vplan-km-proposed>> names the preloads of the Key
Manager tests.

Notes on these lists:

* `sep_cpu_ctrl_err_pulse_probe_o` (Mailbox, IRQ, PIC) and `dma_bus_err_latch_probe_o` (SPI and DMA) give the same observation of the DMA host-path latch.
* The Key Manager contracts of a partial `CMD_KEY_LOAD` failure, a partial `CMD_KEY_TRANSFER` failure, a Key Manager mailbox overflow or underflow, and a Key Manager CPU interrupt line need four injects: a KPV slot-write failure, an engine key-write failure, a Key Manager mailbox error and a CPU interrupt line. No Phase 3 entry uses them, so they are not in this list (<<sep-vplan-phase3-noobs>>).

[[sep-vplan-phase3-added-hooks]]
====== Hooks that the tests add

Each item is an added hook (<<sep-vplan-credit-rules>>): an error inject, which
the test adds to the inject exceptions of `hw/sys/sep/dv/docs/SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc`,
or a top-port drive, which the test adds to the port table of the HDL Top
section of `SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc`. An item of the kind "Existing bench hook" exists
in `hw/sys/sep/dv/tb/tb_top.sv` and `SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc` does not list it: it is
approved, and the test that uses it adds its row to `SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc`
(<<sep-vplan-credit-rules>>). An item of the kind "Existing bench hook, listed"
exists in `tb_top.sv` and in the inject exceptions of `SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc`; the
list names it for its users only, and the test adds no row. An added hook and an
existing bench hook are not PROPOSED and block no check, leg or bin. The column "Port or inject point" names the `sep` port of a top-port
drive (`hw/sys/sep/rtl/sep.sv`) and the point of an error inject. The memory
macros, the eFuse integration and the external TRNG source are in
`hw/top/sep_ip_integration.sv`, outside the `sep` module. The column "Tests that
use it" names the entries whose checks need the hook.
