[[sep-vplan-phase3-proposed-drives]]
====== PROPOSED internal drives

Each item drives, forces or deposits into a net inside the `sep` module for a
purpose other than error injection, and it is not in `SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc` and not
in `hw/sys/sep/dv/tb/tb_top.sv`. A check that needs one is blocked. The column
"Tests that need it" names the entries that carry the blocked check.

[cols="2,3,2,6,6",options="header"]
|===
|Area |Name |Kind |What it drives |Tests that need it
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-key-manager,Key Manager>>
|`HK-STRM-PAT`
|Drive
|`TVALID`, `TDATA` and `TSTRB` of the DRBG sampler stream at the KM sink, with legal beat patterns, in place of the live stream for one read: every non-zero `TSTRB` value, drawn byte values and drawn `TVALID` delays; a partial beat (`TSTRB` 0x1) followed by a stall; one-cycle `TVALID` pulses with `TREADY` high; a complete beat followed by a `TVALID` drop after the handshake.
|`sep_km_drbg_sampler_rand_test` (CHK-SMP-PACK, CHK-SMP-TO-ASM, CHK-SMP-STRM-CTRL)
|===


[[sep-vplan-phase3-oos]]
===== Out of scope

This section lists features outside Phase 3 credit. Each reason names the open
test that owns the feature, or states why no pass or fail value exists.

[cols="3,5,8",options="header"]
|===
|Area |Feature |Reason
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>
|The effect of the `HT_WATERMARK_NUM` selector codes.
|The RDL states the `WATERMARK_TEST` codes REPCNT_HI 0, APT_HI 1, APT_LO 2, MARKOV_HI 3 and MARKOV_LO 4 and the effect that `HT_WATERMARK` shows: a `CTRL.MODULE_ENABLE` rising edge arms a high code to 0 and a low code to 0xFFFF (`entropy_source.rdl`, `HT_WATERMARK`). The open Phase 2 test `sep_esrc_ht_watermark_arming_test` grades this effect for every supported code, the resolution of an unsupported code to REPCNT_HI, and the fall of both low codes. `sep_esrc_csr_rw_walk_rand_test` stores a value of the selector inside its stated range and reads it back.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>
|Each wrapper terminates the key bus in a key CSR block: HMAC, AES and KMAC take 8 words per share, OTBN takes 12, and ABR takes 8 per seed block (four blocks).
|Words per share are graded end to end: a wrong count breaks the delivered-key results of the open sideload tests for HMAC, AES, KMAC, OTBN and the ABR seeds.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>
|Each OpenTitan wrapper path is 64-bit AXI, narrowed to 32-bit AXI, converted to AXI4-Lite, isolated, then bridged to TL-UL.
|The bridge chain is graded by use of every accelerator register in `sep_reg_bit_bash_rand_test` and `sep_hmac_kmac_cpu_crypto_smoke_test`.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>
|HMAC masks its host address to a 13-bit (8 KB) window that holds the register space and the message FIFO window at offset 0x1000.
|The HMAC window width and the FIFO offset are graded by use: a digest needs the FIFO window.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>
|KMAC masks its host address to a 12-bit (4 KB) window before the AXI-to-AXI-Lite stage.
|The KMAC window width is graded by use.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>
|Without SEP_ABR_EN, both the ABR crossbar port and the KM ABR key bus end in error responders that return DECERR.
|`sep_abr_absent_decerr_test`.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>
|The crypto EDN adapter forwards the per-beat FIPS tag to OTBN RND. OTBN RND checks the tag and can set ERR_BITS.rnd_fips_chk_fail. The integration ties the CSRNG seed tag and the external mux tag high, so a high tag only prevents the check from failing.
|`sep_crypto_edn_round_robin_grant_test` and `sep_otbn_compute_rand_test` grade RND consumption and ERR_BITS. The tag is 1 on every path, so a tag of 0 cannot be driven and rnd_fips_chk_fail has no control window.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>
|The OTBN IMEM and DMEM SRAM interfaces leave SEP as four ports: sep_crypto_pka_{imem,dmem}_sram_{req_o,rsp_i}.
|`sep_otbn_mem_smoke_test` and `sep_axi_strobe_window_test`.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>
|OTBN has a 256-bit datapath with a 32-entry register file, a 32-bit control path with a 32-entry register file, flow control, dual flags, modular arithmetic and a TRNG noise stream.
|Descriptive statement of the core datapath. The executed behaviour is graded by `sep_otbn_compute_rand_test`. The register files and flags have no register view.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>
|The OTBN driver library supports ECDSA/ECDH P256 and P384, Ed25519, X25519, RSA 2k/3k/4k and SHA-256/512; microcode loads into SRAM.
|A library statement. The spec gives no vectors and no program images, so no SEP-level pass or fail value exists. The OpenTitan IP-level DV owns the library.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>
|AES supports 128, 192 and 256-bit keys; KEY_LEN is one-hot (AES_128 = 001, AES_192 = 010, AES_256 = 100, reset AES_128).
|`sep_aes_mode_keysize_rand_test` grades the three key sizes in ECB, CBC and CTR.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>
|The AES key CSR block carries 8 words per share. AES passes the two shares into the cipher as a masked pair and does not combine them on the delivery path.
|The sideload tests grade the delivered key through the engine.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>
|KMAC kstrength selects L128, L224, L256, L384 or L512 (reset 0; the codes come from the upstream register description named in the Conventions).
|`sep_kmac_mode_strength_rand_test` grades every legal strength cell.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>
|KMAC CFG_SHADOWED.kmac_en = 1 makes KMAC mode active and prepends the secret key to the incoming message.
|`sep_kmac_mode_strength_rand_test` grades the keyed KMAC digests.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>
|KEY_LEN.len selects Key128, Key192, Key256, Key384 or Key512 (codes from the upstream register description named in the Conventions); key registers hold up to 512 bits per share.
|`sep_kmac_mode_strength_rand_test` grades the five key lengths.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>
|Eleven RW PREFIX registers hold the encoded function-name and customization strings for cSHAKE.
|`sep_kmac_mode_strength_rand_test` grades the PREFIX words through the digest.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>
|KMAC CFG_SHADOWED.sideload (reset 1) selects the delivered 256-bit key (8 words per share). The engine applies the key length itself and ignores the key-length register. A keyed operation with KEY_CTRL.KEY_VALID = 0 raises an error.
|`sep_km_kmac_sideload_kat_test` and `sep_km_sideload_share_walk_test`.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>
|The Key Manager key bus ends in abr_wrapper_key_reg. sep_abr_kv_shim presents the dual-share secrets to abr_top over kv_read/kv_write. Four seed blocks (MLDSA_SEED, MLKEM_SEED_D, MLKEM_SEED_Z, MLKEM_MSG) each have 8 words per share and their own KEY_VALID.
|`sep_km_sideload_share_walk_test` and the Key Manager ABR tests.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>
|ABR reports faults only by interrupt: PIC source 35 (error) and 36 (notification).
|The ABR error and notification interrupts reach sources 35 and 36.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>
|ML-DSA is the post-quantum signature path intended for secure boot. REQUIRED_SIGNERS selects owners and REQUIRED_ALGS selects algorithms per owner. SEP ROM does not use it and verifies one RSA-3072 signature.
|A secure-boot flow statement. REQUIRED_SIGNERS and REQUIRED_ALGS belong to the life-cycle chapter and its bucket.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>
|Source requirements are described by NIST SP 800-90B. Source characterization and health-test thresholds belong to entropy-source qualification, and conditioning does not by itself establish entropy.
|A requirement statement with no stated pass or fail value.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>
|The internal chain is ESRC (ring oscillators, extraction, health checks, SHA-256 conditioning), CSRNG (AES-CTR DRBG) and EDN. The gating health test sees the extracted 32-bit stream before SHA-256.
|A decomposition statement. Each element is covered by its own entry, except the position of the gating test before SHA-256 (<<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>>).
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>
|The health tests are repetition-count, adaptive-proportion and Markov. No bucket test is named.
|States that no bucket test exists. A statement of absence has no observation.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>
|The DRBG is AES-CTR-DRBG (NIST SP 800-90A), built from CSRNG and EDN.
|A construction statement. The CSRNG output is graded against an AES-256 CTR_DRBG golden by CHK3 and CHK4 of the open entropy tests.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>
|The internal path is ESRC to CSRNG to EDN to the three sinks. External data can replace a sink stream after EDN, which bypasses the internal chain.
|The chain from ESRC to the sinks is graded stage by stage.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>
|SW_RESET_N.trng_sw_rst_n resets ESRC, CSRNG, EDN and the DRBG endpoint queues; post-mux adapters and the pool clear while it is active.
|`sep_crypto_per_ip_reset_isolation_test` and `sep_trng_reset_recovery_test`.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>
|Reset and isolation are released together.
|`sep_trng_reset_recovery_test` CHK-TRNG-CLEAR-SYNC and CHK-TRNG-JTAG.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>
|The external TRNG CSR path is outside the internal TRNG reset and isolation boundary.
|`sep_trng_reset_recovery_test` CHK-TRNG-EXTERNAL.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>
|The TRNG reset is not an atomic restart of all entropy consumers.
|The spec states that the TRNG reset is not an atomic restart and states no outcome. A test cannot assert a non-contract.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>
|The seed adapter packs 32-bit words (twelve per 384-bit seed) and attaches the provisional FIPS constant 1. The constant does not measure source quality. Qualification must justify the source separately.
|The seed words and the FIPS constant are graded by CHK3 of the open entropy tests.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>
|CSRNG pulls seeds through the entropy_src_hw_if handshake (es_req, es_ack, es_bits, es_fips); it must not take a partly packed seed.
|CHK3 of the open entropy tests grades the 384-bit seed against the 12-word golden.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>
|The CSRNG and EDN 64-bit AXI4-Lite lane adapter forwards only aligned single-lane accesses with strobe 0x0F or 0xF0 and answers others SLVERR. It holds one downstream slot: a read is taken only when no write half is pending, and a same-cycle read and write give the read first.
|`sep_drbg_axil_adapter_refusal_test`, `sep_drbg_axil_adapter_port_arbitration_test` and the two concurrent AW-then-AR tests.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>
|The pool is filled over DRBG stream 2 through the pool post-mux adapter, which converts to the native EDN fill port of u_entropy_fifo. The DRBG itself has no native endpoint.
|The pool fill path is graded by `sep_entropy_pool_aperture_test` and the CHK5_pool rows.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>
|The BIW stage (Barak-Impagliazzo-Wigderson) combines the 12 byte streams with four GF(2^8) multiply-add units (polynomial 0x1B) into one 32-bit word.
|CHK2 of the open entropy tests grades the compressed words.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>
|DEBUG_CTRL routes one of 256 signals to signal_monitor_o. Both DEBUG_CTRL fields are locked by FIPS_LOCK. SEP leaves signal_monitor_o unconnected and false-paths it.
|`sep_esrc_fips_lock_test` grades the DEBUG_CTRL lock.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>
|The ESRC follows NIST SP 800-90B guidelines and is intended for FIPS 140-2 and 140-3. It includes a configurable SHA-256 whitener that software can bypass. AIS31 is a design intent.
|A statement of intent. The whitener bypass is graded in `sep_esrc_conditioning_matrix_rand_test`.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>
|The Key Manager delivers a value as two 32-bit-word shares whose XOR is the value. Word N carries bits [32N+31:32N]. Both shares are always used, an unwritten share reads as zero, and KEY_CTRL.KEY_VALID qualifies the value.
|`sep_km_sideload_share_walk_test` grades that both shares are used.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>
|ABR takes four independently qualified seed blocks (MLDSA_SEED, MLKEM_SEED_D, MLKEM_SEED_Z, MLKEM_MSG), each 8 words per share with its own KEY_VALID. The ML-KEM seed is D then Z in 512 bits and completes only when both are valid. A seed is used only when its seed read is enabled.
|`sep_km_sideload_share_walk_test` grades the four seed blocks and the D plus Z rule.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>
|Word i of a delivered ABR seed or message is software register index i, with byte 0 of the FIPS byte string in bits [7:0]. MLKEM_SHARED_KEY.KEY[i] holds shared-key bytes 4i to 4i+3 in the same order.
|`sep_km_sideload_share_walk_test` and `sep_km_abr_mldsa_kat_test` grade the word order with a non-palindromic seed.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>
|An OTBN, AES or KMAC software reset cancels that engine's endpoints in the shared crypto EDN adapter from the cycle its paths report isolated until reset releases. A word committed to the engine but not acknowledged is dropped and not given to another client. Other clients are unaffected.
|`sep_crypto_per_ip_reset_isolation_test` CHK-EDN-*.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-fabric-remap,Fabric and remap>>
|Burst-to-single conversion beyond two beats (longer, FIXED and WRAP register bursts)
|An open test grades the stated two-beat conversion. The remainder depends on the unstated response to a burst to a register region outside the crypto region.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-fabric-remap,Fabric and remap>>
|Write strobes at CSRs, slots, SRAM and mailboxes
|An open strobe test grades them.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-fabric-remap,Fabric and remap>>
|Hole responses of the fabric CSR banks and the bank addresses
|An open unmapped-policy test grades them.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-fabric-remap,Fabric and remap>>
|`SEP_LOCAL_BASE_ADDR` legal values
|An open alias test grades the live base. Legal base values are unstated, so no leaf draws them.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-fabric-remap,Fabric and remap>>
|SMC request hang during SMC fuse sense
|The hang is a property of the SMC fabric. The testbench SMC responder is the only SMC, and a hang that the responder models cannot fail the SEP RTL. `sep_fabric_smc_route_test` grades the route.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-fabric-remap,Fabric and remap>>
|Outbound post-remap address match
|An open outbound test grades it.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-fabric-remap,Fabric and remap>>
|Alias remap reset state and valid gate
|An open slot test grades the reset state and an open alias test grades the valid gate.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-fabric-remap,Fabric and remap>>
|Alias remap offset add and carry out of bit 55
|An open alias test grades the offset add. A carry out of bit 55 is unstated.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-fabric-remap,Fabric and remap>>
|Alias remap 4 KB granule and 56-bit expansion
|An open alias test grades both.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-fabric-remap,Fabric and remap>>
|Output remap valid bit and pass-through
|An open outbound test grades them.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-fabric-remap,Fabric and remap>>
|Filter entry counts (16 inbound, 32 outbound)
|An open filter test grades the two entry sets.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-fabric-remap,Fabric and remap>>
|Disabled entry
|An open test grades it. `sep_fabric_filter_match_priority_rand_test` (leg 3) adds the armed-but-disabled sweep.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-fabric-remap,Fabric and remap>>
|Unmatched-traffic policy and DECERR on a blocked transaction
|Open inbound and outbound tests grade both. The beat shape of a blocked burst is an open specification question.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-fabric-remap,Fabric and remap>>
|Read-only `data_bus_width` of the filter
|An open slot test grades it.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-fabric-remap,Fabric and remap>>
|Filter lock on both instances
|Open tests grade it.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-fabric-remap,Fabric and remap>>
|Inbound filter bypass in debug and its reset window
|An open lifecycle gating test grades the bypass differential. The reset window is unstated.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-fabric-remap,Fabric and remap>>
|Reach of the filter CSRs from the external master
|An open rule-matrix check grades it.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-fabric-remap,Fabric and remap>>
|Mailbox aperture decode
|An open mailbox test grades it.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-fabric-remap,Fabric and remap>>
|SPI host past-extent cell and slot tails
|An open unmapped-policy test grades them.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-fabric-remap,Fabric and remap>>
|Bridge fault kinds (in-extent hole, read and write) for HMAC, KMAC and OTBN, the `PERIPH_BUS_ERR_STATUS` latch and the clear of only the named bit
|`sep_cpu_bridge_fault_matrix_rand_test` grades the hole write, the multi-block latch and the clear of only the named bit. `sep_irq_ip_to_aggregator_test` grades the hole read and the single-block latch from it.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-fabric-remap,Fabric and remap>>
|CPU IFU invalid-target fetch, the core-internal ICCM and DCCM path, and the CPU TCM target (DMA only)
|`sep_cpu_demux_unmapped_fetch_test` grades them.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-fabric-remap,Fabric and remap>>
|PIC hole and past-extent cells
|`sep_pic_reg_rand_test` grades them.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-fabric-remap,Fabric and remap>>
|The CPU masters and the DMA issue single-beat transactions only. The System Interface may issue bursts.
|A premise of the stimulus, with no response to check. The System Interface burst is driven by `sep_fabric_extension_port_window_test` and `sep_fabric_row_response_matrix_test`.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-mailbox-irq-pic,Mailbox, IRQ, PIC>>
|Inbound mailbox line `m` to PIC source `m + 1` map
|Graded whole, with the claim ID for all eight channels, by `sep_mailbox_plic_test`. `sep_mailbox_fifo_model_rand_test` uses probe bit `m` as its line and adds the gate, isolation and read-threshold cases.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-mailbox-irq-pic,Mailbox, IRQ, PIC>>
|Source 16 (entropy source)
|Graded by `sep_esrc_alert_delivery_test` (`INTR_TEST` sweep and persistent failure). The cause list is a blocked feature.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-mailbox-irq-pic,Mailbox, IRQ, PIC>>
|CSRNG sources 24 to 27
|Graded whole by `sep_irq_ip_to_aggregator_test` and `sep_pic_irq_source_map_delivery_test`.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-mailbox-irq-pic,Mailbox, IRQ, PIC>>
|EDN sources 28 and 29
|Graded whole by the same two tests.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-mailbox-irq-pic,Mailbox, IRQ, PIC>>
|OTBN done source 30
|Graded whole by `sep_pic_irq_source_map_delivery_test`.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-mailbox-irq-pic,Mailbox, IRQ, PIC>>
|Key Manager error ports
|The Key Manager error ports are Key Manager stimulus. The Key Manager bucket owns the stimulus and samples `km_unrecoverable_err_o` and `km_recoverable_err_o` next to vector bits 30 and 31 (`hw/sys/sep/doc/port_table.adoc`).
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-mailbox-irq-pic,Mailbox, IRQ, PIC>>
|Source 34
|Graded whole by `sep_locked_field_access_irq_path_test` and `sep_efuse_secure_tm_write_lock_test`.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-mailbox-irq-pic,Mailbox, IRQ, PIC>>
|Sources 35 and 36
|Graded by `sep_abr_mldsa_keygen_kat_test` and `sep_abr_mldsa_sign_verify_kat_test`.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-mailbox-irq-pic,Mailbox, IRQ, PIC>>
|Sources 37 and 38
|Graded by `sep_entropy_pool_aperture_test` (flag and cause register). The thresholds are not stated.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-mailbox-irq-pic,Mailbox, IRQ, PIC>>
|Source 40 (token match fault)
|Graded whole by `sep_efuse_rma_token_rand_test` and `sep_efuse_token_match_fault_pic_test`.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-mailbox-irq-pic,Mailbox, IRQ, PIC>>
|Source 41 register-path causes
|Graded by `sep_irq_ip_to_aggregator_test` and `sep_periph_bus_err_misaligned_test`. `sep_cpu_bridge_fault_matrix_rand_test` uses the latch as stimulus, and the SLVERR of the CPU access to an unmapped Secure DMA register offset is a control there.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-mailbox-irq-pic,Mailbox, IRQ, PIC>>
|Bark to NMI to handler path
|Graded whole by `sep_nmi_sanity_test` and `sep_reset_wdt_sanity_test`. `sep_nmi_vector_bark_rand_test` grades the concurrency, free-address and reset-vector cases.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-mailbox-irq-pic,Mailbox, IRQ, PIC>>
|NMI vector lock after the set
|Graded whole by `sep_nmi_sanity_test`. The write of 0 or all-ones to the RW1S lock is graded in `sep_cpu_ctrl_reg_shape_test` (Phase 3, 5.21).
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-mailbox-irq-pic,Mailbox, IRQ, PIC>>
|Memory ECC trap and correctable-count paths
|Graded in `sep_tcm_ecc_cpu_trap_test`.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-mailbox-irq-pic,Mailbox, IRQ, PIC>>
|Bridge fault latch retention across a CPU-only reset
|Graded in `sep_cpu_reset_retention_test`.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-mailbox-irq-pic,Mailbox, IRQ, PIC>>
|The value of `NUM_EXTERNAL_IRQS`, the count of implemented sources, as opposed to the 255 sources that the PIC supports.
|No pass or fail value is stated. Sources 44 to 255 are blocked: the specification does not state the width and the map of the boundary input.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-spi-dma,SPI and DMA>>
|DMA design proposals (inline AES, digest-only write, shared engines, microsequencer)
|The text lists proposals, not behaviour (`dma.adoc`, overview). Nothing to stimulate.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-spi-dma,SPI and DMA>>
|DMA endpoint boot ROM
|The crossbar targets do not include the boot ROM (`fabric.adoc`, Top Level AXI4).
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-spi-dma,SPI and DMA>>
|DMA endpoints crypto region, SMN outbound windows, AXI extension sink
|The sink behaviour is not stated or is adopter-defined (`memory_map.adoc`). The SMN windows need outbound filter setup that the fabric tests own.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-spi-dma,SPI and DMA>>
|DMA clock gate
|The specification states no DMA clock gate field. CLOCK_GATE_CTRL is a placeholder (`sep_cpu_ctrl.adoc`).
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-spi-dma,SPI and DMA>>
|SW_RESET_N reaching SPI or DMA
|The map names the reset targets (`memory_map.adoc`). The reset tests grade the reset control.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-spi-dma,SPI and DMA>>
|DMA address below the local alias window
|The outcome is a fabric contract, graded by the fabric tests.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-spi-dma,SPI and DMA>>
|Second register master on the DMA registers
|One register slave serves every master (`dma.adoc`, overview). No contract differs by master.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-spi-dma,SPI and DMA>>
|ICCM and DCCM sub-word access rule
|Not stated (`cpu.adoc`).
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-spi-dma,SPI and DMA>>
|SPI and DMA aperture walk, local SPI without extension traffic, flash data only through COMMAND and RXDATA
|Graded in `sep_fabric_row_response_matrix_test` (silence sequence, DMA past extent), `sep_unmapped_access_policy_test` (SPI past extent) and `sep_irq_ip_to_aggregator_test` (DMA hole).
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-spi-dma,SPI and DMA>>
|DMA alert leg
|Graded in `sep_pic_pending_map_walk_rand_test`.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-spi-dma,SPI and DMA>>
|DMA bus-error window
|Graded in `sep_cpu_bridge_fault_matrix_rand_test`.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-spi-dma,SPI and DMA>>
|DMA endpoint aliases, DMA outbound user signals, DMA as a local master
|Graded in `sep_fabric_dma_endpoint_matrix_test`.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-spi-dma,SPI and DMA>>
|SPI and DMA reset domain, CPU-only reset, reset after GO
|Graded in `sep_cpu_reset_retention_test`.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-spi-dma,SPI and DMA>>
|`STATUS.BUSY` of the DMA may stay set while hardware handshake mode is active and triggered.
|The text says 'may', so it states no pass or fail value. `sep_spi_dma_handshake_rand_test` does not grade `BUSY` before the `GO` clear.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-mem-boot-reset,Memory, boot, reset>>
|Strap count requirement of the platform
|A platform requirement on strap count. It is not a SEP input count.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-mem-boot-reset,Memory, boot, reset>>
|Strap latch
|The strap latch sits in the SMC GPIO block, outside SEP.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-mem-boot-reset,Memory, boot, reset>>
|BL0 firmware use of the straps
|BL0 firmware behaviour. Boot ROM firmware tests are out of scope.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-mem-boot-reset,Memory, boot, reset>>
|BL0 CPU and PMP setup
|BL0 firmware behaviour. Out of scope.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-mem-boot-reset,Memory, boot, reset>>
|BL0 DCCM use
|BL0 firmware behaviour. Out of scope.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-mem-boot-reset,Memory, boot, reset>>
|BL0 staging and DMA copy
|BL0 firmware behaviour. Out of scope.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-mem-boot-reset,Memory, boot, reset>>
|BL0 payload validation
|BL0 firmware behaviour. Out of scope.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-mem-boot-reset,Memory, boot, reset>>
|BL0 PMP unlock and jump
|BL0 firmware behaviour. Out of scope.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-mem-boot-reset,Memory, boot, reset>>
|BL0 failure handling
|BL0 firmware behaviour. Out of scope.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-mem-boot-reset,Memory, boot, reset>>
|No executable BL1 on failure
|BL0 firmware behaviour. Out of scope.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-mem-boot-reset,Memory, boot, reset>>
|Entropy-source debug pin and `FIPS_LOCK`
|They belong to the crypto and entropy area. No observation point exists in the memory, boot and reset area.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-mem-boot-reset,Memory, boot, reset>>
|`SW_RESET_N` default 0x7E
|Graded by `sep_reset_wdt_sanity_test` (`CHK-SWRST-DEFAULT`). `sep_sw_reset_isolation_matrix_test` reads it as a control.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-mem-boot-reset,Memory, boot, reset>>; <<sep-vplan-phase3-key-manager,Key Manager>>
|Key Manager held after boot
|Graded by `sep_reset_wdt_sanity_test` (`CHK-SWRST-DEFAULT`) and `sep_km_wipe_reset_test` (`CHK-PRE`). Graded whole by `sep_km_mailbox_protocol_rand_test` (CHK-HELD) and `sep_km_wipe_reset_test` (CHK-PRE, CHK-RESET). `sep_km_boot_reset_rand_test` and `sep_km_kmcsr_reset_rand_test` use the reset but grade other contracts.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-mem-boot-reset,Memory, boot, reset>>
|A `SEC_DIS` match does not release the reset
|Graded by `sep_sec_dis_override_test` (`CHK-SENSE-GATED-RESET`, `CHK-RETAIN-RESET`, `CHK-OVR-ALONE`).
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-mem-boot-reset,Memory, boot, reset>>
|Threshold written below the running watchdog count
|The result is not stated (see the blocked list, watchdog threshold rules).
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-mem-boot-reset,Memory, boot, reset>>
|Pet before the bark that prevents the bark
|The pet is not defined (see the blocked list, watchdog pet).
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-mem-boot-reset,Memory, boot, reset>>
|Key Manager reset in the middle of a command or a key transfer
|The Key Manager state belongs to the Key Manager area. `sep_sw_reset_isolation_matrix_test` grades the isolation sequence of the Key Manager domain only.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-mem-boot-reset,Memory, boot, reset>>
|`SOFT_RST_CODE` isolation bypass
|It belongs to the Key Manager area.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-mem-boot-reset,Memory, boot, reset>>
|32-bit half writes and low-word carry of `REFERENCE_COUNTER`
|The RDL states no reload rule.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-mem-boot-reset,Memory, boot, reset>>
|Clock stability under load, ROM burst, ROM latency, SRAM latency, SRAM retention, scratch SRAM ECC, bite threshold below bark, life-cycle escalation, performance
|The specification states no contract for these rows.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-mem-boot-reset,Memory, boot, reset>>
|`clk_wdt_i` is a 200 kHz clock, asynchronous to `clk_i`, that times the watchdog counter.
|The rate is a property of the bench clock, and no DUT output states a pass or fail value for it. The hole "Clock of every flop" and the question "Watchdog count unit" cover the observable part.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-mem-boot-reset,Memory, boot, reset>>
|The core has no instruction cache and runs user mode.
|The absence of an instruction cache is a negative property with no probe. The user-mode part stays with `sep_cpu_isa_pmp_csr_test`.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-efuse-lifecycle,eFuse, lifecycle>>
|ROM decision on CHIPLET_DBG
|The decision is firmware, and ROM firmware is out of scope. `sep_lcc_feat_ctrl_state_matrix_rand_test` grades the feature bit only.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-efuse-lifecycle,eFuse, lifecycle>>
|Adopter extension rule for reserved bits
|No bench-visible behaviour exists in the open design.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-efuse-lifecycle,eFuse, lifecycle>>
|Behaviour that needs more than one LCC-bearing chiplet
|The SEP bench has one.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-efuse-lifecycle,eFuse, lifecycle>>
|Behaviour that needs a non-primary chiplet and the forwarded feature control
|Same limit.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-efuse-lifecycle,eFuse, lifecycle>>
|Effect of `SBOOT_DIS`
|The effect is ROM firmware, and ROM firmware is out of scope. `sep_efuse_field_layout_rand_test` grades the used-bit readback of `SBOOT_DIS`.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-efuse-lifecycle,eFuse, lifecycle>>
|Key rejection by SEP ROM
|Key rejection is firmware. `sep_efuse_field_layout_rand_test` grades the vector as shadow data.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-efuse-lifecycle,eFuse, lifecycle>>
|ROM use of `BL1_VERSION` and `BL2_VERSION`
|The use is firmware. `sep_efuse_lock_slot_matrix_rand_test` grades the flag storage.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-efuse-lifecycle,eFuse, lifecycle>>
|Sense completion triggers memory repair, and when repair is done or bypassed the CPU is enabled and fetches from ROM.
|Memory repair has no register, port or probe at SEP level in the specification or the RDL, so no check can fail on it. The reset-release term (`rst_ni`, `ext_boot_seq_done_i` and sense done) is graded by `sep_boot_release_terms_test`.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-efuse-lifecycle,eFuse, lifecycle>>
|The stage-to-stage required-signer propagation model and its worked examples.
|The specification defers the signing model and states no pass or fail value. `sep_efuse_lock_slot_matrix_rand_test` grades the storage of `REQUIRED_SIGNERS` and `REQUIRED_ALGS`.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-efuse-lifecycle,eFuse, lifecycle>>
|The SEP memory map has one eFuse register aperture (`0x1093_0000`). The Key Manager register reference lists the offsets `0x11000` to `0x11570`, which do not reach the eFuse, because the Key Manager aperture is a 4 KiB mailbox window.
|A document inconsistency with no observable behaviour. `sep_efuse_window_decode_reset_test` grades the eFuse apertures, and the Key Manager tests grade the Key Manager window.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-efuse-lifecycle,eFuse, lifecycle>>
|`SYSCLK_FREQ_MHZ` is a 32-bit field with 11 bits used (MHz). A value of 0 is ignored, and the boot ROM uses the field for clock math.
|The use of the field is ROM firmware, and ROM firmware is out of scope. The specification states no hardware-visible effect at SEP level. `sep_efuse_field_layout_rand_test` grades the used-bit readback.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-key-manager,Key Manager>>
|CMD_HW_VER version value
|The CMD_HW_VER response equal to VERSION 1.0.0 is graded by `sep_km_command_set_rand_test` (CHK-HW-VER). `sep_km_kmcsr_reset_rand_test` grades the VERSION register on the KM bus only.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-key-manager,Key Manager>>
|`test_en_i` selects `scan_rst_ni` in both scan multiplexers of the Key Manager reset conditioner, for the cold and the warm output.
|`sep_scan_reset_override_test` grades both conditioner outputs with `test_en_i` high and low (CHK-SCAN-IDLE, CHK-SCAN-REPLACE). Both checks drive the `sep` inputs `test_en_i` and `scan_rst_ni`, a top-port drive that the test adds (an added hook). They read `km_cond_rst`, an observation tap the test adds to the SEP_TB_ARCH observation-probe list.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-key-manager,Key Manager>>
|The SEP host reaches the Key Manager only through `km_mailbox_sep` at `0x1092_0000`. No SEP map row exposes the KPV, the KMCSR, the DRBG sampler or the key blocks.
|The aperture and the DECERR rows are graded by the open Key Manager aperture contract through the host response. The absence of other map rows is structural and belongs to the fabric decode area (`sep_fabric_row_response_matrix_test`).
|===

'''''

include::test_contracts/smoke_uvm.adoc[tag=sv-uvm-smoke]

[[sep-vplan-formal]]
=== Formal Verification Plan

SEP has no formal flow.

[[sep-vplan-regression-groups]]
=== Regression Groups

The groups below come from `testlists/all.toml`, which includes the per-area
lists. `python3 tools/dv/run_dv.py --dut sep --items <group>` runs a group.
Add `--regress --sim-jobs N --build-jobs N` to fan the leaves out:
`--sim-jobs` defaults to 1, which runs the leaves one at a time, and
`--build-jobs` uses the `--sim-jobs` value when it is not given. Set both
from `nproc`.

[cols="1,3,4",options="header"]
|===
|Group |Tests |Purpose
|`smoke` |`sep_axi_smoke_test` |Pull-request and push gate (`.github/workflows/sim.yml`) on Verilator. It needs no firmware toolchain
|`all` |Every registered test of the Test Procedures (a Phase 3 entry joins when its test is registered), the union of `cpu_stub` and `cpu`; `testlists/all.toml` carries `expected_count` |Full SEP regression and the coverage set. Two members are pinned to one simulator: `sep_km_ic_reset_release_test` runs on VCS only, and `sep_abr_absent_decerr_test` on Verilator only. A single-simulator run logs the other member as skipped
|`cpu_stub` |The `no_cpu` members of `all`, on the CPU-stub target `lsu_stub_all_live` (`sep_abr_absent_decerr_test`: `lsu_stub_abr_absent`) |CPU-stub class with a high simulation fan-out. The scheduled CI tiers run it. Add `--stage c_compile` when the KM `rom_main` images are stale
|`cpu` |The firmware-boot members of `all`, on the full-CPU `default` target |Full-CPU class with a bounded fan-out, because the threaded VeeR EL2 model can stall near reset. Its `c_compile` stage needs a picolibc-enabled RISC-V GCC or `scripts/docker-run.sh`
|`rom_fw` |Boot ROM firmware tests (`testlists/rom_fw.toml`), on the `rom_boot` target |Boot ROM firmware owner. Not a member of `all`, and outside this plan (<<sep-vplan-scope>>)
|`rom_fw_smoke` |`sep_rom_non_secure_boot_test`, `sep_rom_ot_dma_boot_test`, `sep_manifest_magic_survives_handoff_test`, `sep_rom_ot_pio_boot_test`, `sep_rom_ot_secure_boot_test` |Boot ROM firmware smoke subset (`expected_count = 5`). Not a member of `all`
|===

`--items smoke` is the pull-request command. `--items all --tag smoke` is a tag
filter over `all`, not the CI command. A randomized test runs on N seeds in one
regression when its testlist entry sets `reseed = N`. `--stage sim --seed N`
replays one failing leaf.

`--framework uvm --items <group>` runs the members of the group that have a
`uvm` binding; naming a test with no `uvm` binding is an error. `all` holds
every bound scenario (<<sv-uvm-smoke>>).

The pre-merge gate is the class split, run locally:

[source,bash]
----
python3 tools/dv/run_dv.py --dut sep --items cpu_stub --regress \
  --tool verilator --stage flist --stage hdl_compile --stage sim \
  --sim-jobs 24 --build-jobs 24
python3 tools/dv/run_dv.py --dut sep --items cpu --regress \
  --tool verilator --stage flist --stage c_compile --stage hdl_compile --stage sim \
  --sim-jobs 8 --build-jobs 24
----

[[sep-vplan-scheduled-tiers]]
==== Scheduled tiers

The pull-request and push gate (`.github/workflows/sim.yml`) runs `smoke` on
Verilator. Both scheduled tiers in `.github/workflows/regress.yml` run
`--items cpu_stub`, not `all`: the nightly and the weekly tier. Neither tier
collects coverage. The runners
are GitHub-hosted, and the shared `dv-run` action installs uv, Bender and
Verilator only, so a `c_compile` stage has no RISC-V toolchain to call.

A green CI badge therefore says nothing about the `cpu` and `rom_fw` runtime
classes: no CI tier runs them. Firmware-boot evidence comes only from a local
`all`, `cpu` or `rom_fw` run on a host that has the toolchain.

[[known-limitations]]
=== Known Limitations

Each row names a behaviour that the suite does not prove, and what that means
for a reader of a green run. A row is one of four kinds: a hardware or
specification gap, where the RTL and the specification disagree or the
specification is silent; a
documented design limitation; a firmware or integrator constraint, where the
hardware is correct as built and software or the integration must program it
correctly; or a limit of this bench. A firmware constraint is not an open
defect. Behaviour that this plan does not claim at all is in
<<sep-vplan-scope>>.

[cols="1,1",options="header"]
|===
|Limitation |Impact

|Token-comparator common-mode fault (documented design limitation). `hw/sys/sep/doc/token_processing.adoc` ("Matching Logic Countermeasures") states that the redundancy detector does not cover common-mode faults: if all three comparator instances flip the same way, a mismatch can read as a match (fail-open).
|`sep_efuse_rma_token_rand_test` `CHK-COMMON-MODE-MATCH` grades that documented behaviour: a common-mode flip on a wrong RMA_SIP token gives the legal match code, no sticky bit and no interrupt. No check in the suite can detect such a fault.

|Token-comparator collapse inject (`3'b001`). The `tb_top` mode `3'b001` forces instance 0 to a collapsed pair and leaves instances 1 and 2 as a legal match. The fault output is the OR of collapse and disagree.
|`CHK-COLLAPSE` does not prove that the collapse detector exists. Isolating that term needs all three pairs alike and `compute_comparison_vld_i=1`. SEC_DISABLE's valid is the sticky digest valid, not the RMA `digest_vld && fuse_sense_done` term, so an all-alike force with SEC valid still 0 is expected not to raise that rail.

|Inbound CFG allow-list (firmware constraint). Hardware does not hard-block allow-listing the inbound-filter CFG window, outbound CFG, the remap banks, or `SEP_GLOBAL_BASE_ADDR` / `SEP_REGION_SIZE`. The filter applies the rules it is given and is correct as built.
|Firmware must keep those CSRs outside every allow window; allow-listing them is a programming error. The suite proves ownership under correct programming (`CHK-OWNERSHIP`).

|Routed-but-reserved spans (specification). `sep_axi_map_refuse_test` drives reserved addresses and accepts any refusal; it does not grade the code that `sep.rdl` states for each. Named reserved spans are excluded from that walk with a counted reason.
|The refusal code is graded only where `sep_unmapped_access_policy_test` probes. It grades the system-bus reserved span (`0x10A4_0000`–`0x10A5_FFFF`) for refusal, for the code the SEP memory map states, and for no alias.

|KM application ROM wipe paths. Every leaf whose testlist entry declares `firmware = { name = "rom_main", mode = "km_rom_main" }` (`sep_drbg_real_sink_multi_km_aes_test` in `testlists/crypto.toml`; `sep_km_otbn_sideload_kat_test`, `sep_km_aes_sideload_kat_test`, `sep_km_hmac_sideload_kat_test`, `sep_km_kmac_sideload_kat_test`, `sep_km_command_set_rand_test`, `sep_km_wipe_reset_test`, `sep_km_handover_test`, `sep_km_abr_seed_sideload_test`, `sep_km_abr_mlkem_sideload_test`, `sep_km_abr_mldsa_kat_test` and `sep_km_sideload_share_walk_test` in `testlists/km.toml`) boots the KM application ROM from `hw/ip/key_manager/approm/prod` built with `KM_BOOT_WIPE=0 KM_UNREC_WIPE=0` (`cocotb/tests/km_fw/Makefile`). The production default wipes KM SRAM at boot and on an unrecoverable fault. On this bench the boot wipe halts before `RESP_KM_READY`.
|These leaves do not exercise the boot-time and fault-time SRAM shred of the shipped ROM, and no claim here covers it.

|KM CPU bus error visibility. The KM CPU has no bus-error input, so it observes an error response only as the sticky `IRQ_STATUS.AXI_SLVERR` / `AXI_DECERR` bits.
|KM error responses are graded on those bits. `sep_km_key_policy_vault_test` CHK-EXTENT and CHK-LOCKUSE show them set on an erroring store and load while the image runs on.

|KM mailbox outbound flush. `sep_km_mailbox_protocol_rand_test` holds the Key Manager in software reset so that fill, overflow and underflow are deterministic. Only the KM CPU pushes into the outbound FIFO, so that FIFO is empty when `CTRL.FLUSH` runs.
|CHK-FLUSH proves the inbound flush only. A flush of a non-empty outbound FIFO needs the KM running and is not claimed.

|Dead-space per-beat refusal on writes (AXI). AXI returns one `BRESP` for a whole write burst, so a write burst that refused only its tail is indistinguishable from one that refused every beat. Read bursts are proven per beat: the bus monitor keeps the `RRESP` of each beat in order, so `CHK-DEADSPACE-BEAT` can name the beat that answered.
|In the crypto region the write contract is the burst rule: the burst answers DECERR and no register moves (`CHK-DEADSPACE-BURST`). Where the decode of the refusing block is observed, the refusal is named per word: `sep_km_mailbox_protocol_rand_test` CHK-CTRL-EP shows that the KM mailbox register block refuses the upper word of a 64-bit beat at `SEP_CTRL`, at the first offset past its window.

|Entropy pool pointer fault, PIC source 39 (hardware not built). `sep_internal_interrupts[38]` is reserved for the pointer-integrity error of the entropy pool FIFO. The check is not built in this configuration, so the source is tied to zero. `interrupts.adoc` documents the same.
|The source can never assert, and no test grades it. A built check is level-sensitive with no write-one-to-clear, so firmware must mask the source or scrub the pool rather than acknowledge it.

|Single-beat register access (firmware and integrator constraint). `hw/sys/sep/doc/memory_map.adoc` ("Single-Beat Register Access") limits register regions to single-beat transactions. The later beats of a burst to a register region may be answered with errors or alias onto other registers; only the crypto region refuses bursts in hardware. The SEP CPU and the secure DMA issue single beats only, so the system interface is the one initiator that can burst into a register region: through an inbound filter entry with `allow_burst` set, or while `SEP_DBG` bypasses the inbound filter.
|Firmware must not set `allow_burst` on an entry that covers a register region, and system masters must use single beats. No checker grades a burst into a non-crypto register region. `sep_fabric_deadspace_decode_test` grades the crypto-region refusal.

|Dead-space refusal code (reported, not graded). `memory_map.adoc` says that the fabric refuses an address past the extent a unit allocates. `hw/sys/sep/regs/sep.rdl` publishes the past-extent response of each unit, and the generated SEP memory map renders it in its Past Extent column.
|`sep_fabric_deadspace_decode_test` asserts the refusal, not the code: a refusal with any code other than DECERR is reported as `DEADSPACE-FLAVOUR` and is not failed. The test logs each refusal code.

|ABR unpopulated offsets inside the register map complete OKAY (vendored collateral). `hw/sys/sep/doc/adams_bridge.adoc` (aperture-decode paragraph) documents that the vendored ABR register block has no address check: a word access to an unpopulated offset inside the register map completes as the Hole in Extent column of the SEP memory map states, and a partial-word write there answers SLVERR.
|The in-map offsets are not refused: the vendored block cannot refuse them. Past the register map the crypto demux refuses the access, which `sep_abr_access_size_test` `CHK-ABR-UNPOP-RD` / `-WR` / `-PARTIAL` and `sep_fabric_deadspace_decode_test` grade.

|KMCSR extent. `KEY_MANAGER_KMCSR_SIZE` is `0x4C0` (`hw/ip/key_manager/regs/gen/c/key_manager_addr.h`) inside the 2 KB (0x800) decode window that `hw/ip/key_manager/regs/km_csr.rdl` states ("Size: 0x4C0 of registers in a 2KB (0x800) decode window").
|`sep_abr_absent_decerr_test` loads `KMCSR base + 0x4C0` from the KM CPU and logs the answer: AXI_SLVERR with data 0. No checker grades this.

|Adams Bridge absent build. Only `sep_abr_absent_decerr_test` runs on the `lsu_stub_abr_absent` model. Every other leaf runs with `SEP_ABR_EN` defined.
|The ABR memory tie-off and the held-low ABR interrupts of that build have no checker.

|Verilator covergroups. SystemVerilog covergroups are not available under Verilator.
|Feature coverage on Verilator is the feature matrix (<<iconic-scorecard>>). The VCS Phase 1 bins are in <<sep-dv-functional-coverage,SEP_FCOV>>. RTL code coverage is a separate, unthresholded number (<<sep-fcov-evidence-paths>>).

|ROM-FW ownership. Boot ROM firmware contracts belong to the `rom_fw` class, which has its own owner.
|This plan grades none of them. <<sep-vplan-scope>> states why.

|Observation probes. The TB observation and inject ports are the closed set that <<sep-dv-testbench-architecture,SEP_TB_ARCH>> lists.
|No checker observes a behaviour that needs a probe outside that set.

|Behavioural OTP array. The array behind the fuse-command interface is a simulation-only behavioural bank, not an OTP macro.
|Two properties that the eFuse programming entries appear to establish are properties of that model rather than of hardware. Write-one-to-set additivity is OR-only by construction, so a bank that overwrote instead of OR-ing cannot be represented, and no-clobber is not a failable contract. Persistence across cold reset comes from the model having no reset on its storage. What is proven against real logic is that each program reached its addressed bit through the real programming path, and that the sense FSM re-reads the array after reset with the shadow tracking it.

|JTAG MMR token-block access. `hw/sys/sep/doc/otp_fuse_controller.adoc` allows the MMR token block and denies the shadow, the program/read interface, and the SHIM.
|No checker claims DECERR on the MMR token block. `sep_fabric_inbound_filter_rule_matrix_test` `CHK-OWNERSHIP` owns inbound ownership under correct programming (CFG, outbound CFG, remap banks, and region CSRs outside every allow window).

|RTL assertions are backend-dependent. Under Verilator, `prim_assert.sv`, `abr_prim_assert.sv` and `ocah_assert.svh` all gate their macros on `VERILATOR`, so design and vendored-IP SVA expand to nothing, and the SEP Verilator targets pass no `--assert`, so the two-state `ocah_axi_sva` rules are dropped as well. Under VCS those macros are live. The shared native profile also defines `COMMON_CELLS_ASSERTS_OFF`, so the PULP `common_cells` inline assertions are off on every backend.
|Where a checker proves a property, that checker is the whole of the proof on Verilator; no assertion layer sits underneath it there. `sep_km_ic_reset_release_test` is the exception: its contract is the `prim_rom` `noXOnCsI` assertion, so its testlist entry runs on VCS only, and a Verilator regression drops it.

|CPU reset-vector TDR (specification). `hw/sys/sep/doc/cpu.adoc` documents a CPU reset-vector TDR selected by a JTAG opcode. The vendored tap implements no such register: the vector reaches the CPU through a direct wrapper input.
|The testbench shifts that opcode on every CPU-boot run, and the sequence is inert. It is not evidence of the TDR.

|Class 2 scan-root count (specification). `hw/sys/sep/doc/attack_countermeasures.adoc` states "exactly 10" Class 2 scan roots. The RTL instantiates an eleventh on the entropy-pool EDN leg, which is absent from the list, and nothing lints the manifest against the RTL.
|That manifest is the sole input that decides what is never scan-dumpable. No checker grades the manifest.

|`sec_dis` is not forced. No entry forces `sec_dis`.
|The LC→`feat_ctrl` stitch stays an end-to-end path. A forced override is not an acceptable substitute for it.

|Checkers sharing one pass line. The following firmware tests evaluate several conditions and print a single pass line: `sep_spi_ot_dma_rx_test` (3 of 3 checkers), `sep_dma_cpu_contention_test` (5 of 5) and `sep_mailbox_plic_test` (4 of 4).
|Their checker rows do not fail independently of each other. Firmware counters still gate the verdict.

|Lifecycle codes with no owner. `sep_efuse_lcc_lc_state_stitch_test` walks four of the seven named codes on the OTP-program path (`LC_TEST_DEV`, `LC_PROD`, `LC_RMA_SIP_1`, `LC_RMA_CHIP_1`). The `LC_STATE` write-1-to-set leaves add `LC_RMA_SIP_0`, `LC_RMA_CHIP_0` and `LC_PROD_END` as sensed starting states with a `FEAT_CTRL` claim on each, plus two codes outside the named set.
|The encodings are closed. What remains unowned is the *pairing*: `LC_RMA_SIP_0`, `LC_RMA_CHIP_0` and `LC_PROD_END` are sensed but never reached by programming a fuse bit, because the OTP image is a t=0 deposit and a fuse survives reset. From a given image only a W1S superset is reachable in one leaf.
|===
